GRAND MASTER’S DECISION AND RECOMMENDATION
Grand Master Hopper’s Decision No. 1
Alternative Dispute Resolution
We live in a litigious society, and sometimes that litigious nature can
invade the lodge sanctuary of who best can work and best agree. Over the
years, your Grand Lodge leadership has spent countless hours behind the scenes
refereeing personal disputes between brothers. We have seen these disputes
escalate into divisions within lodges and lodge charters being suspended.
Your Grand Lodge leadership continues to work on ways to address
these problems. We are working to train our Inspectors in conflict resolution.
We address conflict in our Wardens Retreats leadership training.
I think that there is another area which needs attention. These disputes
sometimes result in the filing of Masonic charges, based more on some caustic
outburst than any evidence of unmasonic conduct under our Code. Under
Section 1404.320 of the California Masonic Code, these Masonic charges are
sent by the lodge to Grand Lodge, and the Grand Master appoints a Trial
Master. The Trial Master has the power to dismiss the charges if he concludes
that all of the evidence before him does not support a verdict of unmasonic
conduct. In many of these instances, there is no unmasonic conduct, only
personal animosity between two brothers. The Trial Master should dismiss such
charges but to do so often times might further inflame the situation. These
personal disputes go to trial so that each party can have “his day in court.”
Unfortunately, a Masonic trial is not the best forum in which to resolve these
personal disputes, but it sometimes is all that appears available.
I believe that the Trial Master has the power to conduct a mediation
between the antagonists before he renders his decision on whether a Masonic
trial should be conducted. With the help of some lawyers, I have written a
protocol for just such a mediation proceeding. I believe that Trial Masters
should be encouraged to use this mediation procedure when such appears to be a
possibly better initial step than dismissing the charges or proceeding to trial.
It is therefore my decision that the Trial Master may conduct a
mediation of any dispute giving rise to the filing of Masonic charges before he
renders a determination under Section 1404.320 of the Code either to dismiss
the charges or to allow them to proceed to the next step in the trial procedure.
Mediation is appropriate when personal disputes between brothers result in the
filing of the Masonic charges. However, mediation is not appropriate when the
Trial Master finds in the charges evidence which may warrant a verdict of
unmasonic conduct. In that case, the charges must proceed to the next step in
the trial process.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

152

If you approve this decision, the following narrative will be added to
the appendix of the California Masonic Code in which the Grand Master’s
interpretative decisions are recorded:
Interpretation of Section 1404.320 of the California Masonic Code.
Mediation
(2008 G.M. Decision No. 1)
The Trial Master may conduct a mediation of any dispute giving rise to
the filing of Masonic charges before he renders a determination under Section
1404.320 of the Code either to dismiss the charges or to allow them to proceed
to the next step in the trial procedure. Mediation is appropriate when personal
disputes between brothers result in the filing of the Masonic charges. However,
mediation is not appropriate when the Trial Master finds in the charges evidence
which may warrant a verdict of unmasonic conduct. In that case, the charges
must proceed to the next step in the trial process.
The following is a sample protocol suitable for such a mediation
procedure.
A.

The Trial Master shall advise the accuser and the accused
(collectively, the “parties”) that there will be a mediation of
the disputes giving rise to the filing of the Masonic charges.

B.

The Trial Master may act as the Mediator, or he may appoint a
Mediator, who shall be a Past Master in good standing of this
Jurisdiction who is skilled in Masonic Law, from a list
maintained by the Committee on Trial Review. A Past Master
disqualified under Section 1404.320 of the Code from serving
as Trial Master with respect to the charges should not serve as
the Mediator. Acting as the Mediator shall not disqualify the
Trial Master from continuing to act as the Trial Master in the
event the matter is not resolved through mediation.

C.

The Mediator, after consulting with the parties, shall set a time
and place for the mediation. The Mediator shall have sole
authority over the conduct of the mediation. If at any time
during the mediation the Trial Master finds evidence which
may warrant a verdict of unmasonic conduct against the
accused, the mediation must terminate and the Trial Master
must proceed as required under the third paragraph of Section
1404.320 of the Code.

D.

At the mediation, the parties are entitled to counsel as
provided for in Section 1404.540 of the Code.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

153

E.

If the mediation results in a settlement agreement, the same
shall be reduced to writing and signed by the parties, or tape
recorded, with the parties expressing their assent to all of its
terms. The settlement agreement may be of any type or form,
so long as, in the opinion of the Mediator, it does not violate
any provision of this Code or of state or federal law. Upon
the parties reaching a settlement, the Mediator shall cause the
parties to sign a document (the “withdrawal”) which states,
“The charges filed in case number ______ have been
withdrawn.” The Mediator shall immediately forward the
original settlement agreement and the withdrawal to the Trial
Master.

F.

If in the Trial Master’s judgment all of the evidence before
him, taken together, including his consideration of the
mediation proceedings and settlement agreement, would not
warrant a verdict of unmasonic conduct against the accused,
then he shall approve the settlement, dismiss the charges and
so advise the Grand Secretary, who shall so advise the Master
to whom each such charge was originally presented. The
original mediation settlement agreement, the withdrawal and
the dismissal shall be sent to the Grand Secretary. As
provided in Section 1404.320 of the Code, such a dismissal
shall not prevent any subsequent filing of a charge with more
evidence.

G.

If after the Trial Master’s consideration of all of the evidence
before him, including his consideration of the mediation
proceedings and settlement agreement, the Trial Master finds
evidence which may warrant a verdict of unmasonic conduct
against the accused, the settlement and withdrawal must be
disregarded. The Trial Master shall review each charge
referred to him and strike out those portions containing no
clear and precise specifications of an offense, or which are
ambiguous, uncertain, unsatisfactory, or insufficient. After
the Trial Master’s review of all such charges, he shall provide
the Grand Secretary with the charge or charges as they may
have been revised by him. The Grand Secretary shall then
send a notification of the Trial Master’s appointment, together
with each such charge as he has received them from the Trial
Master, to the Master to whom such charge or charges were
originally presented. The consideration of the charges shall
then proceed as provided in Sections 1404.325 and following
of the Code.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

154

H.

If the mediation does not result in a timely settlement
agreement, the Trial Master shall proceed as required under
the third and fourth paragraphs of Section 1404.320 of the
Code.

I.

So that the mediation may be conducted with full and open
discussion, anything said, any admission made and/or any
document prepared in the course of, or pursuant to, mediation
are confidential communications and should not be disclosed
in any Masonic trial or in any civil action or other
adjudicatory proceeding; provided, however, that evidence
otherwise admissible outside of mediation is not made
inadmissible or protected from disclosure solely by reason of
its introduction or use in mediation. The Mediator should not
testify as a witness in any Masonic trial, or in any civil action
or other adjudicatory proceeding, regarding anything said or
done in the course of, or pursuant to, the mediation.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Our Grand Master’s Decision Number 1 recognizes the inherent
authority of a Trial Master to initiate mediation between the parties to a Masonic
trial. Mediation is a non-adversarial approach to conflict Resolution. The role
of the mediator is to facilitate communication between the parties, assist them in
focusing on the real issues of the dispute, and generate options that meet the
interests or needs of the parties to resolve their conflict. It is a system of dispute
resolution that maintains the harmony that is the hallmark of our Fraternity.
Today, mediation is widely used in a variety of disputes, ranging from
divorces to civil lawsuits to very complex public policy issues. Even when
conflicts are seemingly intractable, they sometimes yield to mediation. Because
many disputes occur in the context of relationships that will continue over future
years, a mediated settlement that addresses all parties' interests can often
preserve a relationship in ways that would not be possible in a win/lose
decision-making procedure such as a Masonic trial
Harmony is the strength and support of all societies—especially of
ours. Yet all too often, we have allowed disputes between Brothers to grow to
such proportions that Masonic charges are filed by one Brother against another.
Lost are the Masonic teachings of “whispering good counsel” and “to see who
best can work and best agree.” Currently, thirty pages of the California
Masonic Code are devoted entirely to issues regarding the bringing and trying of
Masonic charges. There is no explicit provision in the California Masonic Code
that calls for any form of dispute Resolution other than having a trial. However,
there are many ways in which disputes may be resolved short of trial. The most
amicable method, a method that encompasses our Masonic ideals, is mediation.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

155

This Decision is consistent with the policy and general purposes of this
Grand Lodge.

Decision AFFIRMED.

Grand Master Hopper’s Recommendation No. 1
Aligning the Inspector’s Term to Coincide
with the Grand Master’s Term
The District Inspector plays a vital role in the leadership structure of
our Grand Lodge. Among his important responsibilities include the selection
and training of Lodge officer’s coaches, qualifying officers for installation, and
representing the Grand Master in all fraternal and administrative matters. A
Grand Master relies heavily on each Inspector and that is why our Code grants
to the Grand Master the duty of appointing the Inspectors who will serve with
him each year. However, the terms of the Grand Master and the Inspector are
different enough that more than a quarter of the Grand Master’s term can pass
before the new Inspector’s term begins. This year, for example, the new Grand
Master’s term will begin on September 28th. The term for the Inspectors
appointed by him will not begin until January 1st of next year. Aligning the
Inspector’s term with that of the Grand Master will help build a strong, united
leadership team for the Grand Lodge.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the California Masonic Code. Section 405.010 of the Code would be
amended to read as marked:
§405.010. APPOINTMENT.
The Grand Master shall divide this Jurisdiction into districts and shall appoint
for each district an Inspector, who shall be a Past Master of this Jurisdiction, and
who shall, before entering upon his duties, produce a certificate from the Grand
Lecturer or an Assistant Grand Lecturer that he is proficient in the work and
lectures adopted by the Grand Lodge and is qualified to act as Inspector. Each
Inspector shall serve for a term of one year, commencing on January 1st at the
close of the Annual Communication following the Grand Master’s installation
and terminating on December 31st of the same year at the close of the following
Annual Communication.
If an Inspector has served nine consecutive terms years, he shall be ineligible for
reappointment until the lapse of one term year, unless the Grand Master
determines his reappointment is appropriate for the good of the Craft.

156

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Our Grand Master’s Recommendation Number 1 aligns the term of
office for the Inspectors with the term of the Grand Master, so that an
Inspector’s term commences at the close of the Annual Communication, the
same time the new Grand Master’s term commences.
This Recommendation is recognition of the benefits that arise from the
alignment of the term of office, as it is the Grand Master who appoints the
Inspectors. Under California Masonic Code section 405.020, the Inspector
“shall be the representative of the Grand Master in his district.” It makes good
management sense to have terms of the Inspector and Grand Master coincide,
and the proposed amendment to California Masonic Code Section 405.010
accomplishes this purpose.
This recommendation is consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Every Inspector is appointed by the Grand Master as his Personal
Representative, and is answerable to him. Currently, an Inspector’s term is on a
calendar year basis, beginning in one Grand Master’s term and expiring in the
term of the next. Clearly, this creates a condition of overlapping authority and
responsibility. It should be noted that the Inspector is the only appointment
which does not align with the Grand Master’s term. Although not likely, this
could lead to divided loyalties and leadership direction. Since the Assistant
Grand Lecturer is also appointed by the Grand Master, this too could cause
disparate managerial guidance.
Each Grand Master has developed a budget for his term. The
overlapping terms destroy the financial accountability for both Grand Masters.
We recognize the transition between two Inspectors could cause an
officer to be judged proficient by one Inspector and installed during the term of
another. We stress that a proficiency by one Inspector remains valid and carries
over even though installed in another Inspector’s term. In the unlikely event
this causes any conflicts it can be easily resolved by the Assistant Grand
Lecturer with the concurrence of the Grand Lecturer.
The Ritual Committee supports this Recommendation.

Recommendation APPROVED.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

157

Grand Master Hopper’s Recommendation No. 2
Renaming Delinquent Dues Committee to
Member Retention Committee
As Grand Master, I have worked with our Membership Development
Committee to focus attention on member retention. Other than membership
losses due to death, Lodges lose members to withdrawal and suspension for
non-payment of dues. The average member who requests a demit from a lodge
in California is 67 years old and has been a member for 30 years. The average
member who is suspended for non-payment of dues is 62 years old and has been
a member for 27 years. Over the last five years, more than 1,000 members over
80 years old have been suspended for non-payment of dues. There are many
factors that contribute to this issue, and a number of initiatives are underway to
address them. Through their work, the Membership Development Committee
observed that our current Delinquent Dues Committee, which every Master
appoints for his lodge, has a larger purpose than one might assume from the
committee name. Currently, the CMC states the duty of the committee as
follows:

“to personally, or otherwise, interview each delinquent
member, ascertain the cause, and work closely with the Master
and officers in a continuing effort to effect the retention of all
deserving members unable to pay”

The Membership Committee believes that the main objective of the
Delinquent Dues Committee under its current definition is membership retention
and that by renaming it so, a better understanding of its purpose will result.
Furthermore, the new name will convey a more positive and less punitive
connotation. I agree with and support their findings and therefore submit this
Grand Master’s Recommendation to change the name of the Delinquent Dues
Committee to the Member Retention Committee.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the California Masonic Code. Section 805.120 of the California Masonic
Code will be amended as marked:

§805.120. DUTIES OF MASTER.
It shall be the duty of the Master to:
A.

Preside at all meetings of the Lodge;

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

158

B.

Confer or have conferred all degrees in strict accordance with the
Ritual;

C.

Give, or have given, in full, the lectures appertaining to each degree, at
the time it is conferred, in accordance with the Ritual;

D.

Appoint those officers as provided in the Bylaws;

E.

Appoint an Auditing Committee and a Delinquent Dues Member
Retention Committee at the Stated Meeting next succeeding his
installation;

F.

Appoint a chairman to coordinate with the Grand Lodge Committees
on Lodge Support and Fraternal Support Services and any Grand
Lodge program charged with Masonic education;

G.

Attest at each Stated Meeting of his Lodge all minutes or records of
proceedings as approved at such meetings by the Lodge;

H.

Superintend the official acts of all the officers of his Lodge and to see
that their respective duties are properly discharged;

I.

Carefully guard against any infraction, by the members of his Lodge,
of its own Bylaws, the California Masonic Code, the General
Regulations of Masonry or any of the rights or duties set forth in the
Ritual; and

J.

Provide for the installation of his successor.

Section 806.000 of the California Masonic Code will be amended as
marked:

§806.000. COMMITTEES.
A.

The Master and Wardens shall be a Charity Committee, and shall have
the power to draw upon the Treasurer for any sum, not exceeding $100
at any one time (or such greater amount as the Lodge may from time to
time provide by a standing resolution), for the relief of a distressed
worthy Mason and the wife, widow or orphan of a Master Mason.

B.

The Master, at the Stated Meeting next succeeding his installation,
shall appoint an Auditing Committee, whose duty it shall be to examine
all accounts presented against the Lodge and the books and records of
the Treasurer and Secretary. Neither the Treasurer nor Secretary may
serve on the Audit Committee.

C.

The Master, at the Stated Meeting next succeeding his installation,
shall appoint a Delinquent Dues Member Retention Committee to

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

159

personally, or otherwise, interview each delinquent member, ascertain
the cause, and work closely with the Master and officers in a
continuing effort to effect the retention of all deserving members
unable to pay.
D.

The Lodge may have such other committees as the Master or the Lodge
may deem proper to appoint. All committees shall be appointed by the
Master, unless the Lodge provides otherwise.

E.

All reports of committees shall be in writing.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Our Grand Master proposes to change the name of the Delinquent Dues
Committee to Member Retention Committee as it appears in sections 805.120
and 806.000 of the California Masonic California Masonic Code.
Retention of members has long been a deep concern of our Fraternity.
The name “Member Retention Committee” has a positive connotation and better
describes the purpose and goals of that committee. The new name would also
act as a mission statement for the committee, to retain as Brothers those who are
currently members of our craft.
This recommendation is consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Recommendation APPROVED.

Grand Master Hopper’s Recommendation No. 3
Defining the Duties of the Fraternal Support Services
and Lodge Support Committees
In 2002, the California Masonic Code was amended to consolidate the
duties of certain committees and create certain new committees. Two of the
new committees were the Fraternal Support Services Committee and the Lodge
Support Committee. Over the recent years, the functions of these committees
have been examined as some of their duties have become more or less necessary
as time has passed. This Recommendation will define the committee duties in
accordance with the current practice as well as the needs anticipated for the
future.
The Fraternal Support Services Committee created the role of Lodge
Ambassador some years ago to act as a liaison between the committee and the
Lodge in promoting important programs that benefit the Lodge and its members.

160

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

This Recommendation refines the size of the committee and adds to the types of
programs the committee will promote such as programs that observe the United
States Constitution and the public schools.
The Lodge Support Committee has been effective in recent years
assisting Lodges requesting help in the areas of leadership development,
financial planning and community awareness. As a result, Lodges have avoided
difficulties and even consolidation by working with this committee in designing
strategic plans and effective programs. This Recommendation refines the size
of the committee and defines its important role as a helpful resource to the
Lodge.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the California Masonic Code. Section 406.040 of the Code would be
amended to read as marked:

§406.040. FRATERNAL SUPPORT SERVICES.
The Committee on Fraternal Support Services shall consist of not less than
fifteen five nor more than fifty fifteen members. It shall develop and implement
strategies designed to promote annual contributions to the Masonic Homes
General and Endowment Funds. The Committee shall:
A.

Provide communications, through presentations and written materials,
to Lodges and other interested parties, to increase awareness and
encourage and promote annual contributions;

B.

Respond to and initiate contact with California Masons who may be
interested in providing major financial support to the Homes and refer
them to Grand Lodge and Homes representatives for further

Section 406.055 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§406.055. LODGE SUPPORT.
The Committee on Lodge Support shall consist of not less than three eleven nor
more than nine twenty members. It shall develop program materials for use by
Lodges in their activities in support of the public schools, the observance of the

shall:

Provide assistance to a Lodge in developing and executing various
leadership development, financial planning and community awareness
programs; and

B.

Undertake such other duties as the Grand Master from time to time
may assign to it.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
In 2002, the California Masonic Code was amended to add two new
committees, the Fraternal Support Services Committee and the Lodge Support
Committee. The performance of these new committees has been evaluated over
the past six years, and our Grand Master now recommends that changes should
be made in the structure and duty statements of each.
Experience, of course, is the best teacher, and our Grand Master has
taken the lessons we have learned since these committees were formed and
recommends the proposed changes for each committee as the product of that
hands-on experience. Under this Recommendation, the number of members of
the Fraternal Support Services Committee would be decreased, and its duties
more carefully defined. The Lodge Support Committee would see its
membership increased and its duties expanded, all for the benefit of our Lodges.

It has long been the policy of this Grand Lodge to aid and support the
constituent Lodges, and to seek new and innovative ways to lend that support.
This Recommendation has been made with that goal in mind.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Recommendation APPROVED.

Grand Master Hopper’s Recommendation No. 4
Hazing
For the past few years, several California Masons have either
witnessed, participated in or experienced candidate hazing. In some instances,
the hazing occurred while the candidate was in the preparation room or in other
areas of the Masonic Center. In others, the hazing came about as an illegal
extension of our degree ritual. The hazing has also occurred during all three
degrees themselves, but primarily during the Second Section of the Third
Degree. Traditional methods of persuasion to correct these abuses have been
met with varying degrees of success and have been in some instances
unsuccessful.
Hazing is intolerable in our fraternity. The Ritual of this jurisdiction as
taught by the Grand Lecturer, Assistant Grand Lecturers and District Inspectors
leaves no room for physical or mental injury or humiliation to a candidate.
Accidents can and do occur during degrees even though the performance of
Ritual work is taught so as to minimize such from happening. Unintended and
unforeseeable injury can occur simply by walking down the street. However,
this is not hazing. Hazing is the intentional infliction of physical or mental
injury or humiliation on a candidate, preliminary to, during or following a
degree, and it will not be tolerated.
So that the relatively few brothers who have been guilty of hazing will
clearly understand that such actions will have serious consequences, I propose
that our law be modified to add all hazing as unmasonic conduct. The most
serious kinds of hazing are Masonic offenses already. Any action during a
degree which is likely to cause serious bodily injury is a violation of our Ritual
and involves moral turpitude. Such action is already unmasonic conduct under
CMC Section 1401.010.C. However, I believe that all intentional inflicted
injury, whether serious or not, is against our Ritual, and our law must clearly
prohibit it also.
If you adopt this recommendation, Section 1401.010 of the Code
would be amended to add as unmasonic conduct any intentional infliction of

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

163

physical or mental injury or humiliation on a candidate, preliminary to, during
or following a degree. Section 1401.020 of the Code would be amended to
make clear that accidental or unforeseeable injury or humiliation to a candidate
will not be considered unmasonic conduct.
If you adopt this recommendation, Section 1401.010 of the Code
would be amended to read as marked:

§1401.010. UNMASONIC CONDUCT.
The following acts constitute unmasonic conduct sufficient to support the filing
of a charge against a Mason:
A.

Any violation, before or after initiation, of the criminal laws of the
United States or of any other governmental entity involving moral
turpitude;

B.

Any violation, before or after initiation, of any portion of the moral
law;

C.

Any action or failure to act in violation of the requirements of this
Code, the General Regulations of Masonry or the Ritual which
involves a breach of faith or other moral turpitude;

D.

Any action or failure to act in violation of the requirements of this
Code, the General Regulations of Masonry or the Ritual, after receipt
from the Grand Master of a notice requiring that Mason to act in
accordance with those requirements;

E.

Intentional infliction of physical or mental injury or humiliation on a
candidate, preliminary to, during or following a degree;

DF.

The following acts committed against a Mason, or the wife, or child, or
widow of such a Mason:

EG.

1.

Use of defamatory, vile, indecent, profane, abusive or
threatening language,

2.

Habitual intemperance and drunkenness,

3.

Personal violence,

4.

Falsehood, treachery and deceit, or

5.

Actions similar to those offenses listed herein;

The following acts committed against his Lodge or the Master thereof:
1.

Misrepresentation of any fact in an application for degrees,
affiliation or restoration,

2.

Attempted resignation from or renunciation of Masonry,

Section 1401.020 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§1401.020. NOT UNMASONIC CONDUCT.
The following do not constitute unmasonic conduct sufficient to support the
filing of a charge against a Mason:
A.

A business dispute not involving fraud or one that involves the
innocent inability to pay a just debt as promised;

B.

Political differences and acts in respect thereto;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

165

C.

Mason in his private capacity and his Lodge or Masonic Hall
Association where a full disclosure of the possible conflict has been
made prior to the transaction; or

D.

Actions allegedly violating the laws of other fraternal or social
organizations, including Masonic Organizations.; or

E.

Accidental or unforeseeable injury or humiliation to a candidate,
preliminary to, during or following a degree.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Our Grand Master recommends that the California Masonic Code be
amended to provide a clearer definition of unmasonic conduct as it relates to
hazing in our Fraternity. He points out that some of this hazing exists prior to
the conferral of degrees, and sometimes during a degree itself. Hazing has no
place in our Craft. The ritual of our Jurisdiction has no room for physical or
mental injury or humiliation of a candidate and is not something that is taught
by the Ritual Committee. The Grand Master, in this Recommendation, has
clearly defined the meaning of hazing and more purposely determined that it
will not be allowed in this Jurisdiction.
The Grand Master correctly points out that in the past, some type of
injury may have occurred during the conferral of the degrees even though the
work is taught to minimize that from happening. The Grand Master makes clear
that accidental or unforeseeable injury or humiliation to a candidate is not to be
considered unmasonic conduct.
This Recommendation is consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Hazing is an occurrence that is familiar to fraternities in the academic
world. It is an event that is totally and unmistakenly unmasonic in nature. It
does not conform to any narratives or floor work of the approved ritual of this
jurisdiction. Whether you are being interrogated by the Marshal on the first
degree, being admonished by the Master to “fear not what man can do unto
you,” or making every effort to conform to the obligations we have all taken
hazing is most definitely inconsistent with the basic principles of the great and
gentle Craft that we all love.
The reality of the environment that we all reside in is tht we are a very
litigious society. The issues of hazing have a potential for extensive and
sustainable financial impact on the lodge, the senior leaders of the lodges, and

166

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Grand Lodge caused by hazing incidents is genuine. It is a crime under the
penal code of the State of California.
Most Worshipful Hopper’s recommendation will clearly satisfy the
concerns for proper Masonic conduct, insure safeguards against possible
litigation, and offer protection to those incidents that are accidental in nature. A
codified interpretation of the hazing will identify and clarify willful hazing as an
act of unmasonic conduct. This will bring clarity and uniformity to our Masonic
Code.
The Ritual Committee supports this Recommendation.

Recommendation CARRIED-OVER.

Grand Master Hopper’s Recommendation No. 5
Grand and Glorious Order of the Knights of the Creeping Serpent
The Grand and Glorious Order of the Knights of the Creeping Serpent
is an organization consisting solely of Master Masons. It confers a degree using
a ritual other than the Ritual of this Jurisdiction. Under Section 1200.015 of the
California Masonic Code, this organization’s Constitution and Bylaws must be
approved by a majority vote of Grand Lodge at an Annual Communication
before any California Mason may be a member. Unfortunately, a number of
California Masons are already members, and Grand Lodge has never given the
required approval. The California leadership of this group did present a
resolution for the required approval before the March 31st deadline for
legislation to be considered at this Annual Communication. The resolution had
the required three proponents from three different lodges. Unfortunately,
through some miscommunication, at least one of the proponents is not a member
of Grand Lodge. Therefore, I was required by Code to declare their resolution
out of order.
So that we are not required to wait another year before considering
their request, I have agreed to submit their request for approval of their
Constitution and Bylaws as part of a Grand Master’s recommendation. I report
to you that their Constitution and Bylaws were submitted to Grand Lodge before
the opening of this Annual Communication.
If you adopt this recommendation, the Constitution and Bylaws of the
Grand and Glorious Order of the Knights of the Creeping Serpent will be
approved under Section 1200.015B of the California Masonic Code thereby
permitting any Mason residing in this Jurisdiction to become a member of such
organization. Grand Lodge action allowing California Masons to be members
of this organization should not be viewed as an endorsement of the Knights of

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

167

the Creeping Serpent. It is simply a grant of permission allowing any interested
California Mason to belong to it.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Grand Master Hopper’s Recommendation No. 5 regarding The Grand
and Glorious Order of the Knights of the Creeping Serpent has been referred to
your Ritual Committee and the following is our report and recommendation.
For the past several years reports regarding the Grand and Glorious
Order of the Knights of the Creeping Serpent, aka “Snakes” have been coming
before this Grand Lodge. These reports and comments have come primarily
from the then Grand Master, Most Worshipfuls David Doan, Fred Sorsabal and
Melvyn Stein all stated, among other comments that the “Snakes” were an
unrecognized organization operating in the jurisdiction of California, and were a
detriment to the Fraternity.

1200.015B of the California Masonic Code requires that the membership of
Grand Lodge approve any such organization.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

In the past, the leadership of Grand Lodge has informed this

Early in this Masonic year, Most Worshipful Hopper with the

My brethren what I am about to relate to you are statements made by a

1.

At some point during the ceremony they were exposed to
physical abuse, body punches, kicks and slaps.

The candidates for the Snake initation were ordered to crawl
about the desert clad only in their underwear.

3.

The candidates were buried in the desert sand up to the

These are the most severe of the stories that have come from these
individuals. You may ask why the individuals responsible have not been
brought up on criminal or at least Masonic charges. The answer is simple. The
Brothers who made this information known to me and others demanded that
their name be kept secret because they are afraid for their safety and that of their
families both in the United States and abroad. These Brothers talked to me and
others “on the square” and we gave our words as Masons that we would not
identify them. My Brothers, verbal threats to intimidate anyone who would
expose the actions of the “Snakes” are only the start. If the “Snakes” beat
and/or torture members who are initiated into the organization, think how far
they would go to protect their criminal activity.
My Brethren, the Grand Master was given a copy of their Ritual and he
has assured us that none of what I have just outlined is included in their written
Ritual. We believe that it is reasonable to assume that to do so would insure that
the organization would never be recognized and rightly so.

CARRY-OVER FROM 2007 ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
Resolution No. 07-01
Allow Inspectors to Vote at Annual Communication
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow each Inspector a separate vote
in Grand Lodge.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Inspectors, acting as they do, as the connection between
constituent lodges and Grand Lodge, are uniquely situated to observe trends
developing within their districts; and
WHEREAS, as a consequence, their close up observation bring a view
that is currently not represented in Grand Lodge. Grand Lodge officers
represent views that are state-wide in perspective, whereas the constituent
lodges represent views that are entirely local in nature. By adding Inspectors to
the voting “mix,” the electorate will then include area representatives that could
serve to better balance legislative actions taken at Grand Lodge; and
WHEREAS, since there are currently 102 Inspectors, and the total
number of voting members is already 1,431, the addition of Inspectors would
not result in a massive change to the electorate, but would, rather, simply serve
to make it better balanced.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 403.075 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§403.075. VOTES.
Except as provided in Sections 403.080 and 403.085 of this Code, the following
shall each be entitled to one vote only:
A.

Elective and appointive Grand Lodge Officers except the Grand Tiler
and the Assistant Grand Tiler;

B.

Past Grand Officers;

C.

Inspectors;

D.

Masters, Senior Wardens and Junior Wardens of chartered Lodges; and

D.E.

Past Masters collectively of each chartered Lodge.

If neither the Master nor either of the Wardens of a Lodge has registered, the
elected representative of the Lodge may cast the votes of Master and Wardens
of the Lodge.

172

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Section 403.080 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§403.080. CUMULATIVE VOTING.
If one or more of the Master and Wardens of a Lodge has not registered, or has
registered and is not present, the senior such officer registered, in addition to his
own vote, may also cast the vote of the absentee or absentees. If an incumbent
officer of Grand Lodge, or a Past Grand Officer or an Inspector is also a Master,
Warden or elected representative of a Lodge, he may cast all votes to which
such positions entitle him, but he shall not be entitled to cast the vote afforded
the Past Masters of a Lodge. If the elected representative is a Past Master but
not an incumbent officer of Grand Lodge, or a Past Grand Officer or an
Inspector, he may also cast the vote afforded the Past Masters of a Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of Resolution Number 2007-01 believe that the
Inspectors represent a more general perspective of California Masonry than do
the individual Lodges. Since their role is to act as a liaison between the Lodges
and Grand Lodge, Inspectors do have an interest in the legislation that is voted
on at the Annual Communication. As such, the proponents believe that
Inspectors should have voting privileges. Furthermore, these proponents
believe that, since the number of Inspectors is only a small percentage of the
total number of potential votes that would be cast, they would not be able, by
themselves, to influence the final outcome in any significant way.
Currently ballots are distributed to all elected Masters, Senior Wardens,
and Junior Wardens who attend Grand Lodge. In addition, the Past Masters
from each Lodge are provided one collective ballot. All current Grand Lodge
elected and appointed officers, with the exception of the Grand Tiler and
Assistant Grand Tiler, have one vote each. Finally, each past elected grand
officer in attendance is given a ballot. Thus, there is precedent already in the
California Masonic Code for men who were either appointed to an office, or
even not appointed or elected to any office in the current Masonic year, to cast a
ballot.
An Inspector is the official representative of the Grand Master in his
particular Masonic District. In this role, he does become, at least in part, the
functional equivalent of a Grand Lodge officer.
There are currently 98 Inspectors in California. As an extreme
example of the influence that they might exert, had all of these Inspectors been
able to vote, and did so in a block for the minority side, approximately 25-30%
of the pieces of legislation that were considered at the 2006 and 2007 Annual

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

173

Communications would have had a different fate. It must be emphasized that,
while this would not be an insignificant effect, this is a worst-case scenario, as
all of the Inspectors would be unlikely to be in attendance and to vote the same
way on every issue.
The Inspectors do bring a different perspective to the voting. Their
primary interest is in a broader area than the individual Lodges, but not as broad
an area as Grand Lodge as a whole. This is a unique position that, at this time,
is not represented by any other individual who is accorded a vote in Grand
Lodge deliberations. In the past, the policy of Grand Lodge has been to obtain
as many perspectives as possible. In this regard, as noted, Grand Lodge is
already providing voting privileges to other individuals who, for the current
Masonic year, are neither appointed nor elected. The addition of Inspectors to
the voting pool would allow their unique perspective to be included in any vote
of the Grand Lodge.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:
The Inspectors are among the most devoted and hardest working
brethren in the jurisdiction. As a representative of the Grand Master, they are in
touch with the fraternity at a level that is unprecedented in scope. Their
interaction with the numerous lodges within their district gives them a broad
balanced perspective of the thoughts and opinions of the Craft.
One of the concerns of the brethren is the balance of vote at our Annual
Communication should the Inspectors receive the vote. The fact is that if the
Inspectors voted as a “block” on any specific issue, which is very unlikely, the
total votes cast would be less than 4% of the total votes available.
The privilege of voting is given to those in leadership and we believe
that Inspectors deserve this privilege. The Ritual Committee supports this
Resolution.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

2008 RESOLUTIONS
Resolution No. 08-01
Permits California Masons to be Regular Members of the
National Association of Masonic Scouters
The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit California Masons to be
Regular Members of the National Association of Masonic Scouters.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 1200.015 of the California Masonic Code
provides that “A Mason residing in this Jurisdiction shall not become a member
of any Masonic Organization unless: A. The Masonic Organization existed in
this Jurisdiction on September 27, 1957; or B. The Constitution and Bylaws of
the Masonic Organization have first been submitted to Grand Lodge for
approval at a subsequent Annual Communication. This section shall not be
construed as a recognition of any organization whether or not in existence on
September 27, 1957; nor shall it be construed as prohibiting clubs organized for
the purpose of instituting a new Lodge or organizations or associations of Past
Masters or Masters and Wardens of a Lodge or Lodges of this Jurisdiction or
organizations or associations of Masons which do not confer degrees, and
except for ceremonies of installation, do not have a ritual other than the Ritual
of this Jurisdiction.”; and
WHEREAS, Section 100.060 of the California Masonic Code defines
“Masonic Organizations” as “any group, chapter, order, club, association or
organization requiring Masonic affiliation as a prerequisite to membership,
other than a Masonic Lodge.”; and
WHEREAS, the National Association
(hereinafter “NAMS”) was formed in 2007; and

of

Masonic

Scouters

WHEREAS, NAMS has been recognized by the Relationships
Division of the National Council of the Boy Scouts of America (hereinafter
“BSA”), and is a part of its National Community Relationships Committee in
which Committee the President of NAMS is a voting member; and
WHEREAS, NAMS now exists to serve the interests of Freemasonry
in general and Lodges in particular in utilizing Scouting as a resource to teach
the youth about whom they are concerned good character consistent with the
tenets of Freemasonry, and to serve Freemasons who register and volunteer their
time in the BSA as adult leaders (hereinafter “Scouters”) for the benefit of youth
and to help promote the tenets of Freemasonry within the BSA; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

175

WHEREAS, Lodges of Freemasons, within and without California
are, or could become if they choose, charter partners with BSA to sponsor
Scouting units (Cub Scout Packs, Boy Scout Troops, Venturing Crews, Sea
Scout Ships), which NAMS will support; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodge of Pennsylvania administers a program
by which Freemasons who are Scouters can be awarded, by their respective rand
Lodges, the Daniel Carter Beard Masonic Scouting Award for their
volunteerism in Scouting, which Award NAMS will promote; and
WHEREAS, Regular Members in NAMS are defined as Master
Masons who are in good standing in a Lodge that is chartered by a regular
Grand Lodge and who are registered adult members of the Boy Scouts of
America, all as more particularly set forth in NAMS’ Constitution, a copy of
which is attached hereto and incorporated by reference, as its governing
document (NAMS has no Bylaws); and
WHEREAS, NAMS does not confer degrees, and except for
ceremonies of installation, does not have any Rituals; and
WHEREAS, regular membership in NAMS is $15.00 annually with
the option of a lifetime membership for $300.00; and
WHEREAS, Freemasons of California would like to affiliate with
NAMS as Regular Members, with prior approval of the Most Worshipful Grand
Lodge, F.& A.M. of California; and
WHEREAS, Freemasons in California could benefit from the purposes
of NAMS and from participation therein without distraction from their
participation in their Lodge or other Masonic Organizations, and California
Freemasonry in general could benefit from the positive publicity resulting
therefrom.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Constitution of
the National Association of Masonic Scouters is hereby approved under Section
1200.015.B of the California Masonic Code, thereby permitting any Master
Mason residing in this Jurisdiction to become a regular member of such
organization.

176

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Resolution 2008-01 has been presented to us by the National
Association of Masonic Scouters (NAMS), which was formed in April 2007. It
held its inaugural meeting on May 30th 2007, in Atlanta Georgia, at which
Masons from thirteen states were represented. A prominent Mason and Scouter
from California has been named to its national board.
The National Association of Masonic Scouters, as the name implies, is
an organization for Masons, and it has been recognized by the National Council
of the Boy Scouts of America. Its Constitution, which has been reviewed in
compliance with CMC Section 1200.015.B, does not contravene any of the
laws, rules or regulations of our Fraternity. NAMS does not have a ritual for
membership and there is no issue regarding our ability to recognize it as a valid
organization. During this past year, several Grand Jurisdictions, including
Michigan, New Jersey, Washington and Vermont, have voted to recognize
NAMS.
The National Association of Masonic Scouters was formed to foster
and develop support for the Boy Scouts of America by and among Freemasons
while upholding the tenets of Freemasonry. This includes, but is not limited to,
encouraging Masonic Lodges and other Masonic organizations to charter and
support local units of Boy Scouts, as many Lodges in our jurisdiction currently
do. NAMS provides opportunities for Masonic fellowship at Scouting events
and encourages the support of the Scouting movement at all levels. This
includes but is not limited to Cub Scouts, Venturing Crews and Sea Scouts, and
seeks to help support these boys and young men with a positive experience.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 08-02
Termination of Dispensation
The proponents of this Resolution seek to change the termination date for
dispensation of a lodge under dispensation.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 801.060 of the California Masonic Code states,
“That the Dispensation for a Lodge shall terminate upon the first day of the
month in which the Annual Communication is held”; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

177

WHEREAS, Section 801.065 states, “If Grand Lodge does not order
the issuance of the Charter, it may in the alternative continue the consideration
of the petition one time to the next Annual Communication, in which event the
Dispensation shall be revived and the Grand Secretary shall return it, together
with the Bylaws, books of records and returns to the Lodge which presented
them. The Lodge may then proceed to work under the revived dispensation,
unless legally revoked, until the first day of the month in which the Annual
Communication is held”; and
WHEREAS, this termination date was established to allow the Grand
Secretary time to review all the records of the lodge under dispensation before
making a recommendation to Grand Lodge regarding granting a Charter to said
lodge; and
WHEREAS, at the 2005 Annual Communication, Section 403.010
was changed to allow the Annual Communication to commence at a time and on
a day within 20 days before or after the second Monday in October; which
change has created a scenario in which a “lodge under dispensation” could NOT
operate for a full month if the Annual Communication is held on the last
weekend in September or October; and
WHEREAS, a lodge should be allowed to operate, even while the
records of the lodge are being reviewed by the Grand Secretary.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 801.060 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§801.060. TERMINATION OF DISPENSATION.
The Dispensation for a Lodge shall terminate upon the first day of the month in
which the Annual Communication is held. The Dispensation shall then be
returned by the Lodge to the Grand Secretary., together with At least thirty days
prior to the first day of such Annual Communication, the Lodge shall deliver to
the Grand Secretary the Bylaws, books of records, returns and itemized list of
receipts and expenditures of the new Lodge to that date. The Grand Secretary
shall examine their records and proceedings and report his findings and
recommendations to the Grand Lodge prior to the close of the next that Annual
Communication. If Grand Lodge does not order the issuance of a Charter to the
Lodge at that Annual Communication, it may in the alternative revive the
Dispensation in which event the Grand Secretary shall return it, together with
the Bylaws, books of records and returns to the Lodge which presented them.
The Lodge may then proceed to work under the revived dispensation, unless
legally revoked, until the first day of the month in which the next Annual
Communication is held.

178

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Section 801.065 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§801.065. ISSUANCE OF CHARTER.
A Lodge under dispensation may petition Grand Lodge for a Charter at anytime.
The petition shall be considered at the next Annual Communication. Upon such
a petition, Grand Lodge may order the issuance of a Charter to the Lodge, and
assign it a name and number on the Grand Lodge registry, if:
A.

The Lodge is found qualified;

B.

The Inspector assigned to the Lodge has certified that the Master and
Wardens are qualified to give the work and lectures in the three
degrees, have each received a Certificate of Proficiency in the
Candidate’s Lecture of the Master Mason Degree and are each
proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the government
of a Lodge; and

C.

The Lodge is clear of all indebtedness.

The Charter shall be issued as of the closing date of the Annual Communication
at which it was granted. The Lodge shall be duly constituted within 90 days
after the issuance of its Charter, or its Charter shall be forfeited. Once
constituted, the Lodge shall be a Masonic Lodge for all purposes and thus shall
have all the rights and privileges thereof and be governed by all the provisions
of this Code governing chartered Lodges.
If Grand Lodge grants the petition and orders the issuance of a Charter to the
Lodge, the Lodge’s Dispensation shall be revived and the Grand Secretary shall
return it, together with the Bylaws, books of records and returns to the Lodge
which presented them. The Lodge may then proceed to work under the revived
dispensation, unless legally revoked, until duly constituted.
If Grand Lodge does not order the issuance of the Charter, it may in the
alternative continue the consideration of the petition one time to the next Annual
Communication, in which event the Dispensation shall be revived and the Grand
Secretary shall return it, together with the Bylaws, books of records and returns
to the Lodge which presented them. The Lodge may then proceed to work
under the revived dispensation, unless legally revoked, until the first day of the
month in which the next Annual Communication is held.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution have recognized a technicality in our
Law that might prohibit a Lodge that is operating under dispensation from

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

179

operating for a full month if the Annual Communication were to be held on the
last weekend in September or in October.
Resolution 2008-02 amends sections 801.060 and 801.065 of the
California Masonic Code to remove this anomaly and makes it clear that the
dispensation for a Lodge shall be valid for a full Masonic year while the Grand
Secretary reviews the records of the Lodge under dispensation.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 08-03
Notification of Suspended Member
The proponents of this Resolution seek to require the Grand Secretary to make
certain notifications upon the suspension or expulsion of a multiple member.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 804.165 of the California Masonic Code requires
the secretary of the lodge suspending or expelling one of their members to
notify the Grand Secretary and all other lodges in which he holds membership;
and
WHEREAS, over time omissions have occurred in lodge records so
that they do not accurately reflect the multiple memberships of all their
members; and
WHEREAS, the result has been that members suspended in one lodge
have not always been reported to the other lodges wherein multiple
memberships are held; and
WHEREAS, this has resulted in a California Mason having been
suspended in one lodge while at the same time remaining in good standing in
other lodges where he held membership; and
WHEREAS, this practice violates Section 804.165 of the California
Masonic Code; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Secretary has records reflecting the multiple
memberships held by all California Masons.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 804.165 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

180

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

§804.165. SUSPENSION OR EXPULSION OF A MULTIPLE MEMBER.
A.

Subject to paragraph C below, suspension or expulsion of a multiple
member by a Lodge in this Jurisdiction shall automatically suspend his
membership or expel him from membership in all Lodges in this
Jurisdiction. The Secretary of the Lodge suspending or expelling such
member shall immediately notify the Grand Secretary, and the Grand
Secretary shall immediately notify every other Lodge in which the
multiple member holds membership. Notification shall be given in like
manner in the case of the reinstatement of such member by the
suspending and expelling Lodge.
Such reinstatement shall
automatically restore him to membership in every other Lodge of
which he was a member in this Jurisdiction, upon payment of any dues
or the performance of any other obligations required for restoration to
membership.

B.

Suspension or expulsion of a multiple member by a Lodge in any other
Jurisdiction of which he is a member shall automatically suspend his
membership or expel him from membership in all Lodges in this
Jurisdiction. If such member is subsequently restored to membership
in the suspending or expelling Lodge, he may also be restored to
membership in this Jurisdiction, upon payment of any dues or the
performance of any other obligations required for restoration to
membership.

C.

Suspension of a multiple member by a Lodge in this Jurisdiction for
non-payment of dues shall become effective only after a sixty day prior
Notice of Intent to Suspend has been sent to every other Lodge in
which the member holds membership. During this sixty day period,
the other Lodge or Lodges may contact the member in an effort to
avoid suspension. If within the sixty day period the member fails to
obtain good standing in all Lodges involved, he may be suspended.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Under current provisions of the California Masonic Code, the Secretary
of a Lodge that suspends or expels one of its members must notify all Lodges in
which that member may hold membership of such suspension or expulsion. The
proponents of this Resolution seek to change California Masonic Code Section
804.165 to mandate that it shall be the duty of the Grand Secretary to notify all
Lodges in which the suspended or expelled member may hold multiple
membership of the suspension or expulsion.
It is the policy of this Grand Lodge to simplify, where possible, the
administrative work of the constituent Lodges. A Lodge secretary may not
know all of the Lodges in which a suspended or expelled member is also a

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

181

member; however, the Grand Secretary has this information readily available
and can make the appropriate notifications more efficiently and effectively.
Under this Resolution, it is still incumbent upon the constituent Lodges to report
to Grand Lodge when a member has been suspended or expelled; but this
Resolution would place the responsibility for maintaining accurate records of
multiple Lodge memberships and for appropriate notifications of suspension or
expulsion in the Office of the Grand Secretary.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 08-04
Installation of Assistant Secretary
The proponents of this Resolution seek to modify the Installation Ceremony for
Constituent Lodges to include the installation of an Assistant Secretary.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 805.320 of the California Masonic Code
provides for a lodge to authorize the Master to appoint an Assistant Secretary as
an officer of the lodge; and
WHEREAS, Section 805.510 of the California Masonic Code requires
all lodge officers be installed as soon as practical after their election or
appointment using the Installation Ceremony for Constituent Lodges; and
WHEREAS, the Installation Ceremony for Constituent Lodges does
not include ritual to install an Assistant Secretary; and
WHEREAS, Section 100.085 of the California Masonic Code defines
the Installation Ceremony for Constituent Lodges as part of the Ritual and states
that it may be modified from time to time by Grand Lodge.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Installation
Ceremony for Constituent Lodges be modified by the Ritual Committee to
include ritual to install an Assistant Secretary.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Our current California Masonic Code specifically enumerates, at
Section 805.320, the duties of the Assistant Secretary, if appointed, as well as

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

182

makes provision for payment of compensation for his services as the Lodge may
direct.
All officers of the Lodge, as per California Masonic Code section
805.510, must be installed “as soon as is practical after their election or
appointment.”
The proponents of Resolution Number 2008-04 propose that the
Installation Ceremony for constituent Lodges be modified by the Ritual
Committee to provide for the installation of an Assistant Secretary. As the
Assistant Secretary, if appointed, is now formally recognized as a Lodge officer,
and all officers are to be installed "as soon as is practical after their election or
appointment," it is in keeping with current law and practices that the Assistant
Secretary shall be installed in his office at the same time as are the other
officers.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Many lodges are now utilizing an Assistant Secretary for their lodge. It
is reasonable that if a lodge chooses to authorize an Assistant Secretary, that
individual should have the privilege of being installed.
Your Grand Lecturer, with the concurrence of the Ritual Committee,
has already prepared the Ritual to be included in the Installation Ceremony and
it will be available for your next Installation, should this Resolution be adopted.
Lodges should be aware that a one-time cost will be incurred to
provide a proper Jewel and Apron for the Assistant Secretary.
The Ritual Committee supports this Resolution.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 08-05
Clarifying Conditions to Install Master, Senior Warden,
Junior Warden and Senior Deacon
The proponents of this Resolution seek to clarify the conditions necessary to
install the Master, the Senior and Junior Wardens and the Senior Deacon.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

183

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Sections 805.520 to 805.550 describe what is necessary
to install the Master, Senior and Junior Wardens and the Senior Deacon; and
WHEREAS, in order to qualify for any of the indicated positions,
some Brethren become confused and interpret these sections to require less than
their intended purposes.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 805.520 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§805.520.
ELECT.

CONDITIONS TO INSTALLATION OF THE MASTER-

Before a Master-elect can be installed, there shall be presented to the Installing
Officer a Certificate of Qualification of the Grand Lecturer, the Assistant Grand
Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is situated, or the Inspector of the
district in which his Lodge is situated, certifying that:
A.

He has personally examined the Master-elect and that he is qualified to
give the Master’s work and lectures of in the Entered Apprentice,
Fellow Craft and Master Mason Degrees;

B.

He has received a Certificate of Proficiency in the Candidate’s Lecture
of the Master Mason Degree; and

C.

He is proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the
government of a Lodge.

Immediately after the installation, the Installing Officer shall endorse the
Master’s Certificate of Qualification and forward it to the Grand Secretary.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 805.530 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§805.530.
CONDITIONS TO INSTALLATION OF THE SENIOR
WARDEN-ELECT.
Before a Senior Warden-elect can be installed, there shall be presented to the
Installing Officer a Certificate of Qualification of the Grand Lecturer, the
Assistant Grand Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is situated, or the
Inspector of the district in which his Lodge is situated, certifying that:
A.

He has personally examined the Senior Warden-elect and that he is
qualified to give the Senior Warden’s work and lectures of the Entered

184

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Apprentice, and Fellow Craft and Master Mason Degrees, and the
Master’s work and lecture of the Fellow Craft Degree;
B.

He has received a Certificate of Proficiency in the Candidate’s Lecture
of the Master Mason Degree; and

C.

He is proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the
government of a Lodge.

Immediately after the installation, the Installing Officer shall endorse the Senior
Warden’s Certificate of Qualification and forward it to the Grand Secretary.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 805.540 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§805.540.
CONDITIONS TO INSTALLATION OF THE JUNIOR
WARDEN-ELECT.
Before a Junior Warden-elect can be installed, there shall be presented to the
Installing Officer a Certificate of Qualification of the Grand Lecturer, the
Assistant Grand Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is situated, or the
Inspector of the district in which his Lodge is situated, certifying that:
A.

He has personally examined the Junior Warden-elect and that he is
qualified to give the Junior Warden’s work and lecture of the Entered
Apprentice Degree, Fellow Craft and Master Mason Degrees, and the
Master’s work and lecture of the Entered Apprentice Degree;

B.

He has received a Certificate of Proficiency in the Candidate’s Lecture
of the Master Mason Degree; and

C.

He is proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the
government of a Lodge.

Immediately after the installation, the Installing Officer shall endorse the Junior
Warden’s Certificate of Qualification and forward it to the Grand Secretary.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 805.550 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§805.550. CONDITIONS TO INSTALLATION OF A SENIOR DEACON.
Before a member can be installed as Senior Deacon, there shall be presented to
the Installing Officer a Certificate of Qualification of the Grand Lecturer, the
Assistant Grand Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is situated, or the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

185

Inspector of the district in which his Lodge is situated, certifying that he has
personally examined the member appointed as Senior Deacon and that the
member is duly qualified to give the Senior Deacon’s work of the Entered
Apprentice, Fellow Craft and Master Mason Degrees and the Senior Deacon’s
work and lecture of the Fellow Craft Degree.
Immediately after the installation, the Installing Officer shall endorse the Senior
Deacon’s Certificate of Qualification and forward it to the Grand Secretary.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to clarify the necessary
conditions and qualifications to install the Master, the Senior and Junior
Wardens, and the Senior Deacon of a Lodge.
It has long been the policy of our Grand Lodge to simplify and clarify
those laws which are ambiguous or lack clarity in order to help guide our
constituent Lodges in their business.
The proposed changes to the California Masonic Code do not, in any
way, change the requirements for the qualification of Lodge officers, but merely
make more clear what those requirements are, and always have been.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:
The Ritual Committee presents this resolution to clarify the language in
Sections 805.520 through 805.550 because, for many years, questions have
arisen as to what exactly “the work” referred to in these sections is. What is
being proposed is, in fact, what is currently expected for an officer to qualify.
We have had brethren progressing through the line that have read the CMC as it
is written currently and the language did not leave them with a clear idea of
what was required. Many came away thinking that they needed to learn less
than what is required and were surprised when their Inspector informed them
otherwise. The new wording should avoid this confusion and make it much
clearer. From a practical standpoint, this resolution does not change what you
need to do to be considered proficient and allow you to be installed as a Master,
Senior Warden, Junior Warden or Senior Deacon, but it does make the wording
clearer. Whether this passes or not, it will not change what you’re required to
do for proficiency, but it will make it clearer to those who refer to it. For this
reason, your Ritual Committee has proposed this resolution.

Resolution ADOPTED.
