Resolution No. 08-06
Lodge Social Funds
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow Lodge social funds to be
administered through the Lodge.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, under Section 809.160 of the California Masonic Code,
funds collected for social purposes are not officially funds of the Lodge, and are
not to be administered through Lodge accounts, but are administered by the
Lodge member or committee collecting them; and
WHEREAS, it seems unnecessary, impractical and imprudent for a
member of the Lodge to bear the burden of having Lodge social funds under his
name and Social Security Number; and
WHEREAS, the Lodge should have control over all funds collected
from its members, including social funds.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 809.160 of
the California Masonic Code which reads as follows be deleted in its entirety:

§809.160. SOCIAL FUNDS.
Funds collected by an officer, committee or member of a Lodge from members
of the Lodge for social purposes of the Lodge or its members are received in
trust as special funds and are not funds of a Lodge. They can only be used for
the purpose for which the funds were collected. Receipts and disbursements
thereof should not be entered in the Lodge books, or deposited in or withdrawn
from the Lodge bank account. No officer, committee or member of a Lodge
shall use any pressure in collecting such funds and all payments and
contributions must be voluntary. The Lodge shall approve a procedure for
receiving contributions, making disbursements, presenting accountings for such
funds, and disposing of any surplus. Surplus may be designated for an object or
purpose which will be to the benefit or welfare of the Lodge and may
accumulate for one year after which time the surplus shall become part of the
funds of the Lodge. All such funds shall be subject to audit annually or more
frequently as the Lodge may direct.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Section 809.210 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

187

§809.210. PERMITTED FINANCIAL SUPPORT.
A Lodge may solicit and receive the following types of financial support only:
A.

Dues from its members and application fees;

B.

Contributions and other financial assistance from its own members;

C.

Contributions and other financial assistance from such persons and
entities (other than its members) as may be approved by the Grand
Master;

D.

Interest, dividend, rental, royalty and other forms of investment
income;

E.

Proceeds from the sale or exchange of assets; and

F.

Other types of support as are specifically authorized in this Article 2.

No other forms of financial support are permitted.
Permitted financial support received, whether for fraternal, charitable, social or
any other purpose, shall be Lodge funds upon receipt.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Section 809.560 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§809.560. SOCIAL AND FRATERNAL EXPENDITURES.
A Lodge may expend Lodge funds for the following purposes, subject to the
following limitations:
A.

A Lodge may expend Lodge funds for purposes of its social and
fraternal activities, promoting fraternal intercourse, community
improvement, its charitable activities and its programs that will benefit
residents of the community in which the Lodge is located. Except for
expenditures from funds collected for specific social purposes as
otherwise provided in this section, tThe aggregate annual expenditures
by the Lodge for the purposes described in this section shall not exceed
thirty percent of the Lodge’s total revenue derived that year from
member dues, application fees and net income from investments.;

B.

Contributions, gifts, bequests and other financial assistance received,
receipts on trust funds, financial support received for the Lodge’s
fraternal operations or charitable activities and profits realized from the
sale of capital assets shall not be included in total revenue for purposes
of calculating the thirty percent limitation.;

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

188

C.

Expenditures of financial support received for the Lodge’s fraternal
operations or charitable activities shall not be subject to this thirty
percent annual limitation when spent as follows:
1.

To pay the expenses of the activity through which such
support was raised, such as the expenses of staging the event
at which the support was received or the expenses of printing
and distributing the publication in which the permitted paid
advertising appeared; or

2.

To pay the expenses of the Lodge’s other social and fraternal
activities, promoting fraternal intercourse, community
improvement, its charitable activities and its programs that
will benefit residents of the community in which the Lodge is
located;

D.

Lodge contributions to the Masonic Homes shall not be subject to this
thirty percent annual limitation.; and

E.

In the discretion of the Lodge, any portion of the annual thirty percent
limitation not expended in one year may be carried forward to future
years until spent.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to amend the California
Masonic Code by eliminating Section 809.160 in its entirety and amending
Section 809.210 regarding permitted Lodge social and fraternal expenditures.
The proponents of this Resolution seek to remove any ambiguity as it relates to
the Lodge’s social funds and more precisely outline permitted financial support
and expenditures. Presently, the social funds collected for Lodge activities are
not to be entered on the Lodge’s books or deposited in or withdrawn from the
Lodge bank account. Thus, a member of the Lodge must bear the burden of
having such funds under his name and social security number. Such funds are
subject to audit annually or more frequently as the Lodge requires; however,
they are not considered funds of the Lodge.
This Grand Lodge, throughout the years, has a history of attempting to
simplify its laws and regulations for the betterment of the Craft and the
operation of the constituent Lodges. This Resolution would clarify the intended
use of social and fraternal expenditures in which a Lodge engages and thus
brings the control of all funds collected from its members into the Lodge. This
Resolution also provides clarification regarding the Lodge’s expenditures for its
many fraternal, social, community and charitable activities.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

189

Report of the Committee on Jurisprudence:
The proponents of Resolution 08-06 seek to allow Lodge social funds
to be administered through the Lodge. This legislation would eliminate the
separate Lodge Social Fund. Thus, if this legislation is adopted, all ticket sale
receipts for Lodge social events would immediately be Lodge funds and would
no longer be held by the Lodge’s social committee. We have been asked how
Lodges can comply with the Grand Lodge alcoholic beverage guidelines if this
legislation is adopted.
Under the CMC, Lodges and Hall Associations may not use funds from
the Lodge’s or the Hall Association’s treasury to purchase alcoholic beverages.
However, the current Grand Lodge alcoholic beverage guidelines allow funds
collected by a Lodge committee from ticket sales to purchase alcoholic
beverages. This provision was consistent with our understanding of California
law when these guidelines were promulgated in 1995. However, the California
Department of Alcoholic Beverage Control’s current interpretation of the law is
different. Under the ABC’s current interpretation of the law, there are only two
ways in which a Lodge or Hall Association can have alcoholic beverages as a
Masonic event: they are dispensed by a licensed caterer or they are donated.
They cannot be bought from the Lodge “social fund.” Therefore, this resolution
will not affect the way in which Lodges should be dispensing alcoholic
beverages at Lodge events.
The Committee on Jurisprudence is working with the Deputy Grand
Master on updated alcoholic beverage guidelines which it is anticipated will be
published soon after the close of this Annual Communication. In the meantime,
regardless of whether this resolution is adopted, you should not have alcoholic
beverages at Lodge events, unless they are donated or are served by a caterer
who holds a liquor license.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 08-07
Administration of a Lodge’s Investment Program
The proponents of this Resolution seek to make changes in the manner in which
a Lodge may administer its investment program.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

190

WHEREAS, the recent modification of the California Masonic Code
made some changes in the manner in which a Lodge’s investment program
could be administered; and
WHEREAS, stability and continuity is critical to the successful longterm management of Lodge assets; and
WHEREAS, certain investments are inappropriate for tax exempt
entities, such as tax exempt investments.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 809.110 of
the California Masonic Code are amended as marked:

§809.110. POWER AND CONTROL OVER LODGE ASSETS.
A Lodge must retain control of and hold title to keep all monies, property and
other assets of the Lodge with the power to acquire, mortgage, sell and convey
property and to invest and reinvest its funds, except as follows:
A.

A Lodge may create a Trust and elect a minimum of three and a
maximum of seven designated members of the Lodge to serve as
trustees who shall hold, manage and distribute those Lodges assets
delivered to them in trust, as follows:
1.

Title to all assets to be held as part of the Trust Estate shall be
in the name of the Lodge or a nominee for the benefit of the
Lodge, as provided in this section;

2.

All net income earned shall be reinvested as part of the Trust
Estate;

3.

The Trust Estate or any portion of it shall be distributed from
time to time only as the Lodge may direct;

4.

The Trustees shall have the power to acquire, mortgage, sell
and convey property comprising the Trust Estate and shall
have the power to invest and reinvest the Trust Estate, if done
strictly in accordance with Section 809.130 of this Code and
other investment guidelines established from time to time by
the Lodge;

5.

The term of office of a Trustee shall be a minimum of three
and a maximum of five years, and the terms shall be staggered
so that the terms of office of no more than two Trustees shall
expire in the same year. A Trustee who has served as such for
two consecutive terms shall not be eligible for election as a
Trustee until the lapse of one year. The terms of office of
Trustees shall expire at until the November Stated Meeting, at

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

191

which Trustees shall be elected to succeed those Trustees
whose terms of office have then expired serve until the next
November Stated Meeting. Trustees in office may be
removed at any time. and Aany vacancy in a Trustee’s
position may be filled by the Lodge. No member may be
elected or continue in office as a Trustee if he acts as trustee
under any other trust agreement by the terms of which the
Lodge has a present or future interest, whether direct or
indirect;
6.

This Trust may be revoked at any time by the Lodge;

7.

The Trustees shall act by majority vote;

8.

The Trustees shall keep books in which all financial
transactions of the trust are correctly and plainly set forth.
They shall render complete written statements of receipts and
disbursements made by them, with vouchers, semiannually at
the stated meeting of the Lodge in August and February in
each year;

9.

The Trustees shall serve without compensation and without
bond;

10.

The Trustees shall at all times be governed by all pertinent
provisions of the California Masonic Code and, where such
Code is silent, by the laws of the State of California; and

11.

The Lodge shall exercise its rights under this Trust pursuant to
the authorization of a majority of the members of the Lodge
present at any duly called meeting of the Lodge at which a
quorum is present, such authorization to be conclusively
presumed by written instruments, executed by the Worshipful
Master or Secretary of the Lodge;

B.

All Lodge’s investments (other than cash, cash equivalents, checking
accounts, savings accounts and certificates of deposit) not administered
by trustees as provided in paragraph A above shall be administered by
an investment committee appointed by the Master. Title to all assets to
be administered by the Lodge investment committee shall be in the
name of the Lodge or a nominee for the benefit of the Lodge, as
provided in this section. The Lodge investment committee shall have
the power to invest and reinvest the assets to be administered by it, if
done strictly in accordance with Section 809.130 of this Code and other
investment guidelines established from time to time by the Lodge;

CB.

Lodge assets may be held in the name of a nominee or in the name of a
custodian nominee as the depository for such securities or in the name
of the Lodge in bank or trust company safe deposit vaults, all as

192

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

selected by the Lodge. A nominee shall always be a bank or trust
company chartered by the United States of America or the State of
California or a securities brokerage firm covered by Securities Investor
Protection Corporation (SIPC); and
DC.

A Chartered Lodge may, after receiving consent from the Grand Lodge
Committee on Masonic Properties, cause the incorporation of a
Masonic Hall Association for the purpose of purchasing, erecting or
maintaining and holding title to real estate for a building for Lodge
uses and purposes and invest Lodge assets in such corporation. A
Lodge or Lodges may hold shares of stock or memberships in such
corporation. Space in such building may be rented for commercial and
other uses consistent with the purposes of the Lodge or the corporation
and not in violation of this Code.

A Lodge shall not incorporate itself. A Lodge shall not establish or form a
foundation, trust, corporation or other entity separate from the Lodge with
power to receive, hold, administer, invest or reinvest Lodge assets, except as
otherwise provided in this section.
A Lodge shall not sell any Lodge assets for less than their fair market value.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 809.130 of the
California Masonic Code are amended as marked:

§809.130. LODGE INVESTMENTS.
Exclusive of a Lodge investment in an incorporated Masonic Hall Association
or any Lodge revolving funds, a Lodge may invest its funds only as provided in
this section. The Lodge’s investments allowed under this section (other than
cash, cash equivalents, checking accounts, savings accounts and certificates of
deposit) shall be administered either by an investment committee appointed by
the Master or by trustees as provided in Section 809.110 of this Code. No
investment committee or trustee administration shall be required for the Lodge’s
cash, cash equivalents, checking accounts, savings accounts or certificates of
deposit.
Through the Lodge investment committee or trustees, the Lodge shall confer
and counsel as to any proposed or contemplated investments under this section
with a Registered Investment Advisor of its choice. A Lodge may freely choose
to accept or reject any and all recommendations made to it by the Advisor.
Alternatively, a Lodge may retain a Registered Investment Advisor operating as
an investment manager who offers standardized portfolios through a managed
investment account.

A Lodge shall not invest any of its funds in any manner or obligate itself in any

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to make changes in and clarify
the manner in which a Lodge may administer its investment programs. If a
Lodge chooses to create a Trust Fund, this Resolution sets a minimum of three
and a maximum of seven Trustees and extends their terms in office from one
year to a staggered three to five year term. This will provide consistency in the
investment strategy of the Lodge and is based on the model used by Grand
Lodge.
This Resolution also clarifies that the assets of the Lodge will be held
in the name of the Lodge or a nominee for the benefit of the Lodge and expands
the potential nominee for the Lodge funds to include security brokerage firms
that are covered by the Securities Investor Protection Corporation which is
appropriate protection for Lodge investments. This Resolution also removes, as
an optional investment within the Lodge’s 20 per cent distribution requirement,
obligations of the State of California or its political subdivisions. In sum, the
proposed changes to the California Masonic Code provide good and safe
investment practices.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Investment:
The Investment Committee of your Grand Lodge has reviewed
Resolution No. 08-07.
The proponents of this Resolution are seeking to give the lodges and
their officers more flexibility in the structure and size of their Investment
Management Team and to institute some widely accepted “best practices” for
the same.
The committee urges your “Yes” vote.

Resolution No. 08-08
Distributions from Life Membership Funds
The proponents of this Resolution seek to change the manner in which the
optional annual distribution from a Life Membership investment account is
calculated.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, widely accepted investment best practices are available to
be applied to the investment of a Lodge’s Life Membership fund and are
allowed under the existing provisions of the California Masonic Code; and
WHEREAS, prudent investment theory endorses a conservative
investment program with an objective of total return, accompanied by a sound
distribution policy.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Section 809.150
of the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§809.150. LIFE MEMBERSHIP FUND.
The original amounts paid for life memberships shall be forever kept and
maintained as and for a separate and permanent Life Membership Fund. A
Lodge may invest its Life Membership Fund as provided in Section 809.130 of
this Code, if such investments are kept separate and apart from all other Lodge
assets. Only interest or dividends paid in cash on An annual amount up to four
percent of the prior year-end fair market value of the assets held in the Life
Membership Fund shall be available for general Lodge use.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Section 809.150 of the California Masonic Code states as follows:
“The original amounts paid for life memberships shall be forever kept and
maintained as and for a separate and permanent Life Membership Fund. A
Lodge may invest its Life Membership Fund as provided in Section 809.130 of
this California Masonic Code.” The last sentence of section 809.150 provides,
“Only interest or dividends paid in cash on the Life Membership Fund shall be
available for general Lodge use.”
Recognizing that interest or dividends may not be sufficient for the
maintenance of the Lodge, particularly in this period of financial distress, the
proponents of this Resolution seek to change the distribution to the Lodge for
general Lodge use to an annual amount up to four per cent of the prior year-end
fair market value of the assets held in the Life Membership Fund. This change

196

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

is consistent with current distribution practices of other charitable organizations.
This Resolution does not change the provision that a Lodge may never
access the original amounts paid for life memberships.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Investment:
The Investment Committee of your Grand Lodge has reviewed
Resolution No. 08-08.
The proponents of this Resolution are seeking to bring current prudent
investment practice into the management of a Lodge’s Life Membership Fund.
Modern portfolio management does not make the historical distinction
between the current income (dividends and interest) and the market value
performance (capita gains and losses) of their holdings, rather the sum of both
being referred to as the “Total Return” of the holdings. Coupled with that,
balancing the need for a current cash distribution from the fund with the prudent
need to preserve the purchasing power of the remaining fund, a sound
distribution policy is essential to the long term purpose of the Life Membership
assets.
The committee urges your “Yes” vote.

Resolution CARRIED-OVER

Resolution No. 08-09
Increasing the Fee Paid by Applicants to the
George Washington Masonic Memorial
The proponents of this Resolution seek to increase the fee paid by applicants to
the George Washington Masonic Memorial.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the George Washington Masonic Memorial located in
Alexandria, Virginia, has continually represented the Masons of the United
States since its dedication in 1932 and has overlooked our Nation’s Capital
exhibiting all facets of Masonry to countless visitors since it’s opening; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

197

WHEREAS, the age of the memorial demands increasing levels of
maintenance and requires an assured level of funding to continue to accomplish
its mission; and
WHEREAS, all Master Masons in the United States are members of
the George Washington Masonic National Memorial Association by virtue of
their membership in a constituent Lodge in their respective jurisdictions; and
WHEREAS, the only source of funding for the George Washington
Masonic Memorial is through donations by Grand Lodges, Lodges and
individuals; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodge of California currently supports the
George Washington Masonic Memorial be a contribution of $5.00 paid by each
applicant for the degrees of Masonry; and
WHEREAS, this donation of $5.00 per applicant has been our practice
and at the same rate for many, many years; and
WHEREAS, applicants for the degrees of Masonry in this jurisdiction
should contribute more than a nominal amount to the future of this magnificent
memorial to our First President whose dedication to Freemasonry set this
country on the path of liberty and freedom.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 804.265 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§804.265.
NATIONAL MEMORIAL AND MEMORIAL TEMPLE
CONTRIBUTIONS.
Each applicant for the degrees shall contribute $15 $5 for the Endowment Fund
of the George Washington Masonic National Memorial and $9 for the
California Masonic Memorial Temple, unless he shall have paid such
contributions with a former application to a Lodge of this Jurisdiction. Any
contribution required under this section shall accompany the application or it
cannot be received by the Lodge. All such contributions shall be transmitted by
the Lodge to the Grand Secretary at the time and in the manner specified on
forms provided for that purpose. The Grand Secretary shall pay the same over
to the Grand Treasurer to be held subject to allocation and payment by order of
the Grand Master.
If the application is rejected or withdrawn, the Lodge shall return any such
contribution paid to the applicant. Such contributions shall be non-refundable
upon such applicant’s election to receive the degrees.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to increase the fee paid to the
George Washington Masonic Memorial by degree-applicants from the current
$5.00 to $15.00.
This assessment was first authorized at the 1947 Grand Lodge Annual
Communication and was set at $1.00. The current fee of $5.00 was established
at the 1980 Annual Communication.
It has been a long standing policy of this Grand Lodge to support the
George Washington Masonic Memorial. It is not unreasonable to understand
that costs for the maintenance of the Memorial have increased over the lapse of
time from 1980, making this proposed increase in funding necessary.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Resolution No. 08-10
Voting by Absentee Ballots
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish a procedure whereby lodges
can vote on proposed legislation by absentee ballots.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 403.065 of our California Masonic Code dealing
with the subject of the manner of voting at Communications of this Grand
Lodge permits voting by voice or hand as well as by written ballot; and
WHEREAS, because of the increased travel costs, hotel or housing,
meals, gas, air fares, many lodges cannot afford to send their Master, Wardens
or even an Elected Representative to Grand Lodge Communications; and
WHEREAS, many lodges therefore are not able to have a voice in the
business of the Grand Lodge to which they belong; and
WHEREAS, absentee ballots are commonly used in local, State and
Federal elections and such procedure, if available in respect to legislation before
our Grand Lodge would permit participation by all lodges.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 403.065 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

199

§403.065. MANNER OF VOTING.
The vote on all resolutions, recommendations and reports required to be
presented at least 180 days prior to the commencement of the Annual
Communication shall be by written ballot. All other questions in Grand Lodge,
except election of officers, shall be decided either by voice, hand, a rising vote
or by written ballot, as the Grand Master shall direct, unless, before the
announcement of the result thereof, three members shall demand that the vote be
taken by written ballot. At least one member demanding the written ballot must
have the authority to speak for the others joining with him in the request, and
this shall be so stated in the request for the written ballot. At least one
designated spokesman must be present in Grand Lodge at all times that a written
ballot is to be taken pursuant to the demand. If no spokesman is present, the
Grand Master may in his discretion proceed as though no demand had been
made.
A written ballot is defined as one inscribed upon a slip of paper, or a computer
punch card provided by the Grand Secretary, and distributed, collected and
counted by appointed Grand Lodge tellers. A written ballot shall also include
an absentee ballot described in Section 403.075 of this Code, distributed and
collected by the Grand Secretary and counted by appointed Grand Lodge tellers.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Section 403.075 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§403.075. VOTES.
Except as provided in Sections 403.080 and 403.085 of this Code, the following
shall each be entitled to one vote only:
A.

Elective and appointive Grand Lodge Officers except the Grand Tiler
and the Assistant Grand Tiler;

B.

Past Grand Officers;

C.

Masters, Senior Wardens and Junior Wardens of chartered Lodges; and

D.

Past Masters collectively of each chartered Lodge.

If neither the Master nor either of the Wardens of a Lodge has registered, the
elected representative of the Lodge may cast the votes of Master and Wardens
of the Lodge.
The Master, Senior Warden, Junior Warden and Past Masters collectively of a
chartered Lodge may cast absentee ballots on all resolutions, recommendations
and reports required to be presented at least 180 days prior to the

200

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

commencement of the Annual Communication using the following procedure.
Absentee ballots shall be prepared by the Grand Secretary. Absentee ballots
may be requested by vote of the Lodge only. They shall be requested by the
Lodge Secretary from the Grand Secretary at least forty-five days before the
opening of the Annual Communication. The request shall include a certification
that the persons for whom the absentee ballots are requested are unable
physically to attend Grand Lodge. The absentee ballots shall be sent by the
Grand Secretary to the Secretary of the Lodge requesting them. The Lodge
Secretary shall distribute the absentee ballots to the persons entitled to vote
them. The issued absentee ballots must be returned to the Grand Secretary
before such opening of the Annual Communication to be counted. Each
returned absentee ballot must be accompanied by a certification as to the person
casting the ballot signed both by the person casting the ballot and by the Lodge
Secretary. The Lodge Secretary shall affix the Lodge seal to his certification. A
fee of $5.00 per lodge shall be collected for the privilege of voting absentee by
any one or more persons. If an absentee ballot has been mailed for a particular
officer’s vote, the Grand Lodge tellers shall not issue to anyone a written ballot
for that officer on the same matters. If an absentee ballot has been mailed for
the collective vote of the Past Masters of a chartered Lodge, the Grand Lodge
tellers shall not issue to any Past Master of that Lodge the Past Masters’ written
ballot on the same matters.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish a procedure
whereby Lodges can vote on proposed legislation by absentee ballots, thereby
relieving Lodge representatives of their duty to attend the Annual
Communications of their Grand Lodge.
Since the formation of our Grand Lodge, the constituent Lodges have
been required to send personal representation to each Annual Communication.
In early days, these representatives traveled by foot, horseback and by carriage,
regardless of personal hardship or expense. The honor of representing one’s
Lodge members for debate on issues, balloting on Resolutions, Decisions and
Recommendations, and for electing the new leadership of our Fraternity was its
own reward.
The functions of Grand Lodge with respect to proposed changes in our
Constitution and Ordinances, and the oversight review of a Grand Master’s past
actions, parallels the functions of the United States Congress, which has not
found it wise to allow the use of absentee ballots in performing its duties and
responsibilities. There is more to our Annual Communications than balloting on
issues brought before members of Grand Lodge. It is not uncommon for
emergent issues to be brought before the members, and all present benefit by the
discussions on the floor, in committee meetings and reports, and in the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

201

interpersonal relations developed at the Annual Communication. If that
personal input is lost, not only will it diminish the quality of our Grand Lodge
business, but it will deprive our constituent Lodges of the input they receive
from their fellow officers or representatives from around the state.
The policy of full personal Lodge representation and participation of
each constituent Lodge has withstood the passage of time and has served our
membership well.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED

Resolution No. 08-11
Restoration of Aaron Shubb to Masonic Membership
The proponents of this Resolution seek to restore Aaron Shubb to membership
in Masonry.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 1404.820 of the California Masonic Code
provides that a Mason who has pleaded guilty or nolo contendere to, or has been
convicted of a crime of moral turpitude by a federal or state court shall be
deemed automatically expelled from membership in any lodge in this
jurisdiction without any further action of his lodge or Grand Lodge; and
WHEREAS, Aaron Shubb was automatically expelled from
membership in this jurisdiction because in 1999 he pleaded guilty to a charge of
possession of narcotics with the intent to sell (the “Crime”); and
WHEREAS, Aaron Shubb may be restored to membership in Masonry
by a resolution presented and adopted using the same procedure as an
amendment to the Ordinances of Grand Lodge; and
WHEREAS, Aaron Shubb did not sell narcotics, but rather pleaded
guilty to the charge, on advice from counsel, as part of a plea bargain for a
reduced sentence; and
WHEREAS, after Aaron Shubb performed the required rehabilitation,
the judge in his case set aside the original judgment against him and entered a
plea of not guilty into the court records; and
WHEREAS, Aaron Shubb’s actual crime of possessing narcotics for
personal use is neither a crime against humanity nor a crime of moral turpitude;
and

202

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

WHEREAS, after nine years of being clean and sober, after
volunteering extensively in the drug rehabilitation community, including
speaking to large groups of addicts and young people on the dangers of drugs,
after being an active and engaged leader in his community and in his lodge,
Aaron Shubb currently demonstrates the high moral values and good character
we expect and admire in Masons.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that any provision of the
California Masonic Code to the contrary notwithstanding, Aaron Shubb be
restored to the rights and benefits of Masonry as of the date of his expulsion,
with no lapse in membership, and that he no longer be subject to expulsion from
Masonry under the California Masonic Code because of the Crime.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
In accordance with Section 1404.820 of the California Masonic Code,
which requires the automatic suspension of any Mason who had pled guilty or
nolo contendere to a crime of moral turpitude, Aaron Shubb was automatically
expelled from Masonry in 2007. This section is a logical extension of the fact
that Masons answer to a higher standard.
The proponents of Resolution 08-11 seek to restore this man into
Masonry. CMC Section 1404.1050 does provide a mechanism for restoring an
expelled Mason; this process requires a two-thirds affirmative vote by the
former brother’s lodge before such an application can be presented to Grand
Lodge. Grand Lodge may then restore the man “if good cause is shown”.
The determination as to whether or not good cause exists is initially the
job of the lodge making the application and ultimately that of Grand Lodge.
While California Masonic Code section 1404.1050 does not define what that
good cause is, the former brother’s Lodge voted 40 to five, or 89 per cent, in
favor of reinstatement at its July 2007 stated meeting
It is now up to the Brethren assembled to determine whether Aaron
Shubb should be restored to membership, in keeping with the Masonic value of
assisting a fallen brother and in accordance with Section 1404.1050 of the
California Masonic Code.
This resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Jurisprudence:
The proponents of this resolution seek to restore Aaron Shubb to
membership in Masonry.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

203

Under Section 1404.820 of the California Masonic Code, a Mason who
has pleaded guilty or nolo contendere to, or has been convicted of, a crime of
moral turpitude by a federal or state court is automatically expelled from
membership in any Lodge in this Jurisdiction without any further action of his
Lodge or Grand Lodge. Aaron Shubb was automatically expelled from
membership in this Jurisdiction because in 1999 he pleaded guilty to a charge of
possession of a controlled substance with the intent to sell. In the Committee’s
view, this is a crime of moral turpitude within the meaning of our law.
Therefore, Aaron Shubb’s automatic expulsion for his conviction of this crime
was required by our law.
Aaron Shubb may now be restored to membership in Masonry only by
a resolution presented and adopted using the same procedure as an amendment
to the Ordinances of Grand Lodge. Resolution 08-11 is legally sufficient to
restore Aaron Shubb to membership in California Masonry should it be adopted
with the required vote.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED

Resolution No. 08-12
Restoration of James R. Paden to Masonic Membership
The proponents of this Resolution seek to restore James R. Paden to
membership in Masonry.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 1404.820 of the California Masonic Code
provides that a Mason who has pleaded guilty or nolo contendere to, or has been
convicted of, a crime of moral turpitude by a federal or state court shall be
deemed automatically expelled from membership in any Lodge in this
Jurisdiction without any further action of his Lodge or Grand Lodge; and
WHEREAS, James R. Paden was automatically expelled from
membership in this Jurisdiction because, on or about October 22, 2000 while
under the influence of an alcoholic beverage and a drug, he drove an automobile
which resulted in the death of his wife and bodily injury to his daughter (the
“Crime”); and
WHEREAS, James R. Paden may be restored to membership in
Masonry by a resolution presented and adopted using the same procedure as an
amendment to the Ordinances of Grand Lodge; and

204

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

WHEREAS, in November 2007 James R. Paden was expelled based
on the belief that he was convicted in April 2001 of Vehicular Manslaughter, an
offense of Moral Turpitude; and
WHEREAS, James R. Paden was not, nor ever has been, convicted of
Vehicular Manslaughter.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that, any provision of the
California Masonic Code to the contrary notwithstanding, James R. Paden be
restored to the rights and benefits of Masonry as of the date of his expulsion,
with no lapse in membership, and that he no longer be subject to expulsion from
Masonry under the California Masonic Code because of the Crime.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek reinstatement to our fraternity
of James Paden, who was expelled pursuant to the automatic expulsion
provision of California Masonic Code section 1404.820. The proponents of this
Resolution contend that our Grand Master made a mistake when he issued the
automatic expulsion letter. However, the Jurisprudence Committee has
determined that, under the California Masonic Code, the expulsion letter from
the Grand Master was properly issued.
California Masonic Code section 1404.1050 details the technical steps
required for the reinstatement of an expelled member. But in order for this
Grand Lodge to assess properly whether an expelled Brother should be
reinstated, it needs to consider whether that individual has been rehabilitated
from his criminal conduct and is once again worthy of being a member of our
ancient and honorable fraternity. This Resolution is not premised upon a
demonstration of Mr. Paden’s rehabilitation and presents nothing from which
this Grand Lodge might conclude that his reinstatement should be granted.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Jurisprudence:
The proponents of this resolution seek to restore James R. Paden to
membership in Masonry.
Under Section 1404.820 of the California Masonic Code, a Mason who
has pleaded guilty or nolo contendere to, or has been convicted of, a crime of
moral turpitude by a federal or state court is automatically expelled from
membership in any Lodge in this Jurisdiction without any further action of his
Lodge or Grand Lodge. James R. Paden was automatically expelled from
membership in this Jurisdiction because, on or about October 22, 2000 while

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

205

under the influence of an alcoholic beverage and a drug, he drove an automobile
which resulted in the death of his wife and bodily injury to his daughter. In the
Committee’s view, this is a crime of moral turpitude within the meaning of our
law. Therefore, James R. Paden’s automatic expulsion for his conviction of this
crime was required by our law.
James R. Paden may now be restored to membership in Masonry only
by a resolution presented and adopted using the same procedure as an
amendment to the Ordinances of Grand Lodge. Resolution 08-12 is legally
sufficient to restore James R. Paden to membership in California Masonry
should it be adopted with the required vote.

Resolution No. 08-13

Resolution No. 08-14
Racial and Ethnic Reconciliation
The proponents of this Resolution seek to redress alleged past racial and ethnic
discrimination.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, since time immemorial, our fundamental Ancient
Landmarks have been the brotherhood of man in the fatherhood of the EverLiving God, and the immortality of our Souls; and
WHEREAS, it is the internal characteristics, a man’s character; not
the external, a man’s physical appearance, which we look for in a man if he is to
be made a Mason; and
WHEREAS, first among the Principle Tenets of Freemasonry is
Brotherly Love which states, “By the exercise of Brotherly Love, we are taught
to regard the whole human species as one family, the high and the low, the rich
and the poor, who, as created by one Almighty Parent and inhabitants of the
same planet, are to aid, support, and protect each other. On this principle,
Masonry unites men of every country, sect, and opinion and causes true
friendship to exist among those who might otherwise have remained at a
perpetual distance”; and
WHEREAS, the effluvia from the brutal and heinous form of slavery
practiced primarily in the southeastern part of our country, but was also
extensive in California during the nineteenth century well into the 1860’s and

206

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

1870’s, permeated our entire society for centuries and still we suffer from its
deleterious effects; and
WHEREAS, slavery engulfed the native Indian population before and
after the genocide of the 1850’s, as well as the many African Americans brought
here as slaves by southerners drawn to the gold rush and as this permeated
California culture at the time, many Masons may necessarily have been
involved; and
WHEREAS, the unjust and brutal racial segregation that appeared in
most of the former slave states after the Civil War continued well into the
twentieth century, persisted to a considerable extent in California for a period of
time, particularly with regard to its indigenous population, and is reflected in the
absence of ethnic minorities, particularly African Americans, in “mainstream”
California Masonry until very recently, decades after this pattern of
discrimination began breaking down in the rest of California society; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodge of California did not recognize the
Masonic brotherhood of the Prince Hall Masons in its jurisdiction until 1995, a
process which, once undertaken, took 5 years of negotiations between this
Grand Lodge and the Prince Hall Grand Lodge of California to accomplish; and
WHEREAS, racism has no place anywhere on our earth and especially
not in our Fraternity based on universal brotherhood through the Fatherhood of
God; and
WHEREAS, racism has long divided the body of Freemasonry in the
United States into two groups, those presently members of the Conference of
Grand Masters of North America and those in Prince Hall Masonry; and
WHEREAS, the Government of Australia in 2008 apologized to its
native population for all the suffering the native aboriginal people had endured
over the years that was legally sanctioned and carried out by the Government of
Australia; and
WHEREAS, the 16 million members strong Southern Baptist
Convention, at its sesquicentennial meeting in 1995 adopted a resolution that
condemned racism, apologized to the African-American Community for
Southern Baptist Convention members’ and some member churches’ past
participation in slavery and segregation, and asked to be forgiven by their
African-American brothers and sisters.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that we, as Masons and
“good men and true,” denounce racism, ethnic discrimination and discrimination
of any kind as deplorable.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that we, as Masons and “good men
and true” lament and humbly apologize for the past acts of any “mainstream”
Masonic brethren in California who participated in slavery, the oppression of

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

207

the African-American Community, the horrific genocide and enslavement of the
indigenous California peoples, and the exclusion of African-Americans and
other ethnicities from so many facets of American life, including “mainstream”
Masonry.

208

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that we, as Masons of the Grand
Lodge of California offer, to all African-Americans, our brothers in Prince Hall
Masonry past and present, and men of all ethnicities who found the door of
“mainstream” Freemasonry closed to them for no other reason than their race or
ethnicity, our humble apology.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this body should commit itself
to the eradication of all vestiges of racism in this Grand Lodge and its
constituent Lodges.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that we ask forgiveness, however
inadequate this may now seem, from our brothers in Prince Hall Masonry for
nearly a century and a half of exclusion and discrimination, as well as from
those of other ethnicities who were also excluded.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Grand Secretary send copies
of this resolution to the remaining California Indian Reservations and
Rancherias and to all Prince Hall Grand Lodges whose regularity is now
recognized by this Grand Lodge, or may in the future be recognized, within 30
days of the conclusion of this Annual Communication or within 30 days after
the Grand Secretary is notified of recognition.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Grand Secretary be
provided the necessary extra support staff hours to prepare, copy, and send the
above required copies in the time specified.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution would have our Grand Lodge issue
apologies to a variety of racial and ethnic groups for past “horrific genocide,”
“enslavement” and exclusion “from so many facets of American life.” They
point out that one foreign government and one American religious group have
issued apologies for their past repressive and segregationist practices.
However well intentioned the proponents of this Resolution may be,
they ignore the fact that neither Freemasonry in general, nor this Grand Lodge
in particular, is a political or religious institution. While we practice the three
principal tenets of brotherly love, relief and truth, we neither preach nor govern.
This proposed Resolution has elements of both preaching and governance.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

209

Resolution No. 08-15
Expand Recognition of Prince Hall Masonry
The proponents of this Resolution seek to provide for future recognition without
further vote of this Grand Lodge of each Prince Hall Grand Lodge in the same
manner that it is recognized by the Grand Lodge sharing the same territory.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, this body has offered recognition to Prince Hall Grand
Lodges recognized by the “mainstream” Grand Lodges which share the same
territory, resulting in the recognition of twenty-six Prince Hall Grand Lodges, in
addition to the California Prince Hall Grand Lodge, with visitation and Masonic
communication between our Lodges and theirs; and
WHEREAS, 40 of the 51 Grand Lodges in the United States that are
members of the Conference of Grand Masters of North America have
recognized the regularity of at least the Prince Hall Grand Lodges in their
respective jurisdictions and nearly all allow for visitation and Masonic
Communication; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodges of Illinois, Connecticut, Nebraska,
Colorado (includes Utah & Wyoming), Washington, (all in 2002), Idaho (2003),
New Jersey, District of Columbia (2004), New Hampshire (2006), and possibly
Hawaii, Montana and others have extended “blanket” recognition to all Prince
Hall Grand Lodges recognized by other “mainstream” Grand Lodges and at
least three provided for future recognition and at least two expansion of
Masonic relationships (data based on the web site devoted to Prince Hall
Masonry Recognition Details of Paul M. Bessel, formerly Executive Secretary
of the Masonic Leadership Center); and
WHEREAS, had men of African ancestry been allowed access to
“mainstream” Masonry in the first place, access that was denied for almost two
centuries, Prince Hall Masonry would not have been needed and would
probably not exist today; and
WHEREAS, the regularity of Prince Hall Masonry has been
acknowledged by the United Grand Lodge of England, the Grand Lodge of
Austria, the Regular Grand Lodge of Belgium, the Grand Lodge of France, the
United Grand Lodges of Germany, the Grand Lodge of the Netherlands (Grand
Orient), the Grand Lodges of Ireland and Scotland, the Grand Lodges of the
Philippines and Japan, five Grand Lodges in Australia, and eight Grand Lodges
in Canada; and
WHEREAS, the United Grand Lodge of England had found the
formation of the Mother Grand Lodge, the Prince Hall Grand Lodge of
Massachusetts, to be of “acceptable regularity” and that the “philosophy and

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

210

practice of Prince Hall Masonry today are of exemplary regularity;” and
WHEREAS, additional Prince Hall Grand Lodges may be recognized
in the future by the “mainstream” Grand Lodges which share the same territory,
thereby permitting recognition by our Grand Lodge also.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that this body again
offer recognition to Prince Hall Grand Lodges recognized by the “mainstream”
Grand Lodges which share the same territory which were previously contacted
but have not responded to our prior offer.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that a new Section 409.035 be
added to the California Masonic Code to read as follows:

§409.035.
LODGES.

RECOGNITION OF OTHER PRINCE HALL GRAND

This Grand Lodge shall recognize each Prince Hall Grand Lodge with
jurisdiction over area exclusively outside of the State of California during the
time that each of the following conditions is satisfied:
A.

Such Prince Hall Grand Lodge is recognized by the Most Worshipful
Prince Hall Grand Lodge Free and Accepted Masons of California.
During the time that the relationship between these two jurisdictions is
something other than recognition as understood in this Code, our
relationship with this Prince Hall Grand Lodge shall be the same as that
between it and the California Prince Hall Grand Lodge.

B.

If such Prince Hall Grand Lodge has jurisdiction over area which is
also within the jurisdiction of another Grand Lodge which we
recognize, then these two Grand Lodges recognize each other and
permit concurrent jurisdiction over that area. During the time that the
relationship between these two jurisdictions is something other than
recognition as understood in this Code, our relationship with this
Prince Hall Grand Lodge shall be the same as that between these two
jurisdictions. If such Prince Hall Grand Lodge has jurisdiction over
area which is also within the jurisdiction of two or more other Grand
Lodges which we recognize, all of these Grand Lodges recognize each
other and permit concurrent jurisdiction over that area. During the
time that the relationship among two or more of these jurisdictions is
something other than recognition as understood in this Code, our
relationship with this Prince Hall Grand Lodge shall be the same as the
most restrictive relationship between them.

C.

Such Prince Hall Grand Lodge recognizes our Grand Lodge on the
same basis that recognition is hereby extended to it. The offer of
recognition shall be extended by the Grand Secretary to such Prince

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

211

Hall Grand Lodge within 30 days of his receipt of information that the
conditions of paragraphs A and B of this Section 409.035 have been
satisfied. If the nature of the recognition permitted under paragraph B
is expanded because of actions of other Grand Lodges, then the Grand
Secretary shall offer to expand similarly our recognition of such Prince
Hall Grand Lodge within 30 days of his receipt of information that
such expanded recognition is permitted.
The effect of recognition under this section is also governed by Section 409.020
of this Code.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to expand the recognition of
Prince Hall Masonry by making it mandatory for this Grand Lodge to recognize
every Prince Hall Grand Lodge with jurisdiction over areas outside of California
once certain conditions, as specified in the Resolution, have been met.
It has never been the policy of this Grand Lodge to extend “blanket
recognition” to any group of related, but independent, sovereign jurisdictions.
Whether or not recognition should be given to a Grand Lodge in another
jurisdiction has always been within the discretion of the members of this Grand
Lodge upon the application for recognition by that foreign Grand Lodge.
Making mandatory the recognition of any foreign Grand Lodge would deprive
the members of this Grand Lodge of the ability to make an independent
assessment of the merits of such recognition.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED

Acacia Creek Board of Directors
By Larry L. Adamson, President
BACKGROUND
In 2000, the Board of Trustees of the Masonic Homes of California
launched a strategic planning process for the organization arising out of our
commitment to our historical mission and the need to develop a long-term,
sustainable financial strategy. This was undertaken out of the recognition that
the Homes needs to increase sources of revenue in addition to investment
earnings on its endowment and general funds and address the challenges of
health care inflationary rates which are 2.5 times greater than standard inflation.
A part of this strategic planning process included:



A written survey in 1999 completed by 2438 fraternal members



Focus groups in 2001 attended by 184 members



Meetings with Trustees and Executives from three other Masonic Homes
(Ohio, Pennsylvania and Connecticut) to discuss their growth strategies



12 informational sessions conducted in 2005 and attended by 448
members



Membership demographic analysis and projections



Research into state and federal industry trends

From this research, the Board learned that many members of our aging
fraternity were struggling with the lack of affordable options for residential
services in California. The Board also learned that – all other things being equal
– 98% would prefer Masonic-sponsored services.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

215

THE VISION FOR THE ACACIA CREEK COMMUNITIES
After four years of research, the Board of Trustees arrived at a concept
for creating two new Masonic senior living communities on our existing
campuses. Both campuses had a significant amount of excess land. Creating
new retirement communities on this land would create a revenue producing
asset for the Masonic Homes as well as a new source of service and security for
our fraternal family.
These new communities were both planned as Continuing Care
Retirement Communities (CCRCs) which means that they provide an active
vibrant community for independent seniors as well as more advanced levels of
care - should the need arise.
The new communities were named Acacia Creek at Covina and Acacia
Creek at Union City and admissions to these communities are offered to
California Master Masons, their wives, widows and mothers.
The design and program plans for Acacia Creek at Covina includes 112
new independent residences and Acacia Creek at Union City includes 152 new
independent residences. On both campuses, residents will be offered a variety of
apartment floor plans – ranging from one bedroom apartments of 845 sq feet to
two bedrooms with den cottages of 1650 square feet. The communities will
include new dining rooms, fitness centers, indoor and outdoor pools, a variety of
recreation spaces, and separate Assisted Living and Memory Support care.
Masonic Homes of California will always be open to all eligible Masons –
regardless of their financial assets.
Acacia Creek residents will be expected to financially qualify for
admission. Residents will pay a refundable entrance fee and an ongoing monthly
fee.

ORGANIZATIONAL STRUCTURE
The mission of the Acacia Creek (AC) corporations is to serve
members of our fraternal family and support the mission of the Masonic Homes
of California.
Two separate corporations were created and granted tax exempt
501(c)3 status. Masonic Homes of California is the sole corporate member of
each Acacia entity and elects the Acacia Creek boards, subject to approval by
the Grand Master.
The Board of Directors for each of the Acacia Creek corporations is
identical and includes seven members:

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

216

a representative of the Executive Committee of Grand Lodge:
Larry Adamson, President of the Board,
four overlapping members of the Masonic Homes Board of Trustees:
Michael Neben, Vice President of the Board
Mark Gibson
George Geanoulis
Frank Loui
and two trustees from outside the MHC Board of Trustees:
Loyal Stark (lodge #243)
Ara Maloyan (lodges #307/780/848)

THE DEVELOPMENT PROCESS
The process for development of the Acacia Creek projects was
designed in a manner that allows the Board to minimize our investment while
we test the membership’s interest and demand for such projects. As one
Masonic Homes Trustee aptly put it, “Our approach is not ‘if we build it they
will come – rather we will build it IF they come.” The development process is
structured in phases. Depending on the success of each phase, the Board then
votes to move forward or abandon the projects.

Phase One – the Preliminary Feasibility Stage – was completed.
During this phase, we:



Conducted focus groups



Completed a demographic review and exhaustive competitive analysis
on retirement services already available in California



Completed conceptual master-planning and design work



Developed proposed program guide, pricing plan, marketing plan and
budget



Completed initial financial feasibility studies



Met with City Officials and submitted land use applications

The results of this phase demonstrated significant interest on the part of
the membership and that the available land offered us the opportunity to
affordably build new communities on our existing campuses. With this, the
Trustees voted to enter into the next phase of development.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

217

Phase Two – The Marketing Excitement Period – was completed in December
2006. During this phase, we accepted $1000 priority reservation deposits from
fraternal members interested in residency. We set a goal of receiving 200
priority deposits for each campus and achieved this goal. With this affirming
accomplishment, the Board approved advancing to phase three. During phase
two the membership expressed a great interest and excitement in these projects.

Phase Three – Pre-sales, Design, Entitlements, Financing – was projected to
last 12-18 months. Four key milestones were required and accomplished during
Phase Three:



Received state review and approval of project feasibility



Attained 70% presales per site (10% deposits)



Completed architectural design and secured entitlements from the local
Cities



Secured an initial guaranteed maximum contract with the General
Contractors.



Secured the tax exempt financing for AC Union City and plan to close
on the AC Covina tax exempt financing in November 2008.

With the successful completion of the four key milestones, the Board
approved AC Union City to move forward to phase four. At the time of the
writing of this report, AC Covina had successfully completed three of the four
key milestones and is working on the necessary due diligence to close on the tax
exempt financing.

Phase Four – Construction, Move In, Stabilized Occupancy
The groundbreaking for AC Union City was celebrated in September,
2007. The grading and construction period is scheduled for 22 months.
Residents will start moving in the late Fall of 2009. The groundbreaking for AC
Covina is scheduled for November 16, 2008. The grading and construction
period is planned for 22 months. Residents will start moving in Spring of 2010.
The Board of Directors of Acacia Creek, as well as the Trustees and
Executive staff of the Masonic Homes of CA, are excited by the potential of
these new communities. Four years of research has led us to this point and we
believe that these communities make sense for fraternity and for the Masonic

218

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Homes. These communities will generate revenue to support the charitable
mission of the Masonic Home and serve more of our members in need.
Through the new Acacia Creek corporations, we will be able to serve
an additional 400 elderly members of our fraternal family. In addition, the
Acacia Creek projects will generate revenue for the Masonic Homes of
California through land leases and management fees.
Given that these projects allow us to put unused land to work for us –
and work for our fraternity – and that they fill an unmet need among our
constituents, while generating revenue to support the charitable mission of the
Masonic Homes, we are excited by our prospects. Should the membership
continue to respond so favorably, we look forward in the future when we stand
before two new communities built by and for our Masonic brethren.

Admission Committee
By Gary A. Yanes, Chairman
The Admission Review Program of the Board is a carefully developed
analysis and evaluation process that continues to be improved and refined to
meet the evolving needs to our Applicants, as well as the Homes Staff and the
Board.
The interview process and on site evaluation is done at the same time
the applicant visits one of the two facilities. A Staff report on this overnight
visit, together with the Medical Director’s comments on his/her review of the
medical information, is then added to the application. The staff then develops
an executive summary on the applicants and presents that to the Admission
Committee for review and approval. Anytime the Staff recommends against
admission the full application is given to the Admission Committee for detailed
review and discussion during the Admission Committee meeting. Any
recommendation against admission requires the full Board confirmation.
Prior to the regular Admission Committee meeting on the second
Tuesday of each month, the seven Trustees, who form the Admission
Committee, each receive and carefully study the complete applications not
recommended for Masonic assistance and the executive summaries for
applications recommended for Masonic assistance. Each Trustee reviews and
evaluates the applications and accompanying data and then discusses his
viewpoint during the Admission Committee meeting. Home’s Staff and the
Administrators attend Admissions Committee meeting to answer committee
member’s questions and to provide additional information to the committee
members. The committee’s recommendations and comments are then presented
to the full Board for confirmation.
The number of applications handled by the Admission Committee for
couples, individuals and children continues to vary from month to month. From

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

219

July 1, 2007 to June 20, 2008, 63 have been admitted to both campuses. Union
City currently has 255 residents, including the 15 residents in the Tradition
Special Care Unit. Covina currently has 76 residents. For this time period we
had 115 applications being completed by our brothers, widows, and mothers.
The Masonic Outreach Services program continues to grow and is an
effective program in assisting our eligible Brethren and their families or
widows. This program provides the necessary support, financial, housing and/or
care management assistant to applicants who are in need of this type of
assistance to remain in their present community or residence. The Masonic
Outreach service program is currently serving the need of 181 clients and two
who are on outplacement. This year the committee has reviewed and approved
40 applications for this program. Members of the staff monitor those
individuals on a regular basis to ensure that their needs are being met.
The Children’s program continues to develop and currently has 33
children; 18 boys and 15 girls are housed at the Covina facility. This year we
are seeing a lot of changes in this program as the Board has named Steffani
Kizziar the Executive Director over the Children’s Program. We expected to
see great changes in the future.
I want to thank the other Trustees of the Admission Committee who
labor long and hard over the applications. They are Vice President of the Board
of Trustees, Ken Nagel, Al Gates, Bill Bray, Gary Charland, Frank Loui and
Tim Wood. I would also like to express my appreciation to the Executive
Directors of Covina and Union City Homes and there staff, as well and the
continued dedication of the Masonic Outreach Staff to the applicants and Board
of Trustees.

Children’s Committee
By Frank Loui, Chairman
It has been a busy and successful year in our Children and Family
Services program at the Masonic Homes of Covina. We had a change in
leadership and Steffani Kizziar was named Executive Director of Outreach and
Children’s Services and Sabrina Montes, MSW was named the Interim
Administrative Director. Additionally, this committee recommended and the
Board of Trustees approved a new Clinical Director position for which we are
actively recruiting.
The Children’s Program continues to follow the “Circle of Courage”
program model, which focuses on a holistic approach to developing healthy,
productive children and adolescents. The focus is on fostering belonging and
healthy relationships, teaching the mastery of skills particularly in academics
and special interests or talents, developing self-sufficiency and independence,
and encouraging generosity so that children learn to give back to their

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

220

communities.
Through this new philosophy and model of care, Building Skills for
Life, the children in our care continue to make significant improvement. 17
children passed their classes with C’s or better and as a result, were eligible for
the Mosaic Lodge sponsored trip to Colorado. Those children not eligible took
house vacations to Camp Land in San Diego.
Summer offered children who had poor grades during the school year
the opportunity to make up some classes. We are now tracking all high school
students to ensure they are eligible for the post high school institution of their
choice. Our tutors are also working with all juniors and seniors to prepare them
for the California High School Exit Exam, the PSAT’s, SAT’s, and ACT’s.
During July and August, 2008, the children enjoyed a wonderful
summer day camp. During the morning, the children were involved in oncampus activities, which included arts and craft projects such as t-shirt design,
model car making, latch work, and sand art. Additionally, the children were
involved in team building activities, sports, and pool time. A couple from the
senior community taught two ceramics classes each week in which the children
painted ceramic pieces and completed free-form works.
The afternoons provided the children with opportunities to go on
community outings and participate in campus competitions. The children
participated in a fishing trip sponsored by L.A. Rod and Reel, Guitar Hero 3
tournament, Dance Revolution tournament, roller skating, ice skating, chalk
drawing contest, Build a Bear workshop, Soak City, Bowling, indoor skydiving,
movies, Medieval Times, Sports Museum, Rock Climbing, and Speed Zone.
Although their days were busy with activities they still managed to give back to
the community. They volunteered throughout our facility helping the seniors,
and they also made blankets, cards and care packages for the soldiers stationed
in Iraq. Summer culminated with the campus-wide Summer Olympics with a
variety of competitions and the End of Summer Camp Pool Party with the
Adopted Grandparents.
Through our Independent Living Skills training, the children are
acquiring skills to help them successfully reintegrate into their families or their
communities. The Independent Living Skills training focuses on academic and
job preparation and career placement. Demonstrating the success of this
program, nine former residents were approved for scholarship support.

Finance Committee
By George L. Geanoulis, Chairman
It has been an honor and privilege to serve as Chairman of the Masonic
Homes of California Finance Committee. This committee monitors and manages
the finances for the Masonic Homes of California and is charged with the
important responsibility of ensuring that we serve our residents today, while
protecting our legacy for future generations. These are solemn obligations,

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

222

which I and every member of my committee, take very seriously. The primary
role of the Finance Committee is to review and recommend approval to the
Board of Trustees of the operating and capital budgets and to monitor actual
performance against those approved budgets. Other important responsibilities of
this committee include:


Reviewing recommendations from the Investment Committee of Grand
Lodge regarding investment strategies for Homes’ funds as well as
their recommendations for spending guidelines that establish the
parameters for the operating and capital budgets.



Working with recommendations from the Insurance Committee of
Grand Lodge to establish financially sound risk management and
coverage structures.



Reviewing and approving the reports from external auditors as well as
special internal audits that are conducted as appropriate.



In collaboration with the Strategic Planning Committee, overseeing
long-term financial planning as well as the review of financial analysis
relating to new programs, operational initiatives and capital projects.



Advising the full Board as to the appropriate sources of funding for all
proposed projects and programs.

The Masonic Homes of California ended the fiscal 2008 with a
favorable budget variance of $2.782 million, of which $874K related to a
favorable revenue variance due primarily to resident asset income but also
favorable results in Pharmacy, Rehab Services, Pension Income & Annual Fund
Contributions. The remaining $1.908 million favorable variance related to
strong expense controls and lower than anticipated expenses. These results were
provided using the un-audited financials for the Masonic Homes of CA.
For the past several years, operating expenditures of MHC have been
guided by spending parameters set as a percentage of a five-year rolling average
of the investment balances of the general and endowment funds. This approach
insures our operating budget will be somewhat buffered from significant year
to-year market swings while protecting the integrity of the fund balances for
future generations of residents. This committee is committed to constantly
reevaluating the operations of the Homes to ensure we are operating efficiently,
all the while maintaining the highest level of care and resident satisfaction. In
addition, we continue to explore opportunities to diversify the revenue sources
of the Homes to better protect our assets for future generations.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

223

The Finance Committee reviewed regular reports from the Grand
Treasurer. These reports include quarterly investment performance reports,
monthly operating statements, quarterly capital project reviews and various
others. Moss Adams, LLP the auditors for Grand Lodge and the Masonic
Homes, will be completing the annual external audit for the fiscal year ended
June 30, 2008 in the next 30 days and will be reporting their findings at a
meeting in late October.
The Finance Committee worked with management to develop the fiscal
year 2009 budget using a zero-based approach. In the face of ever rising costs,
management proposed, the Finance committee recommended and the Board
approved a budget which came in at 4.31%, below the 5.0% spending guideline.
The Finance Committee also worked with management to develop a capital
project budget to assure the Homes remain well-maintained and safe to protect
the comfort and safety of our residents. The Homes continues to fund the
Capital Reserve Fund from annual depreciation through the operating budget.
This fund is used to pay for capital projects and to build a reserve for future
needs. The budget developed for FY2009 will allow the balance in this fund to
grow for future capital needs.
During the course of this year, this Committee also oversaw the
development and implementation of several new and important initiatives and
accomplishments, which include:


In the course of preparing for construction of the Acacia Creek Senior
Living Communities, we obtained, for the first time ever, a credit rating
for the Masonic Homes of California. I am happy to report that Fitch
Ratings Agency gave us a score of AA-, making us the highest rated
senior living community in the United States. Standard and Poors
subsequently gave us an AA rating, confirming our status and credit
worthiness.



In February, 2008 Acacia Creek Union City bond financing was
completed in the amount of $93.625 million with a 30 year term. An
interest rate swap was also placed in service fixing our cost of finance
at 3.1% for the next ten years. Interest only will be paid on these
obligations until the project is complete with full operations up and
running. At that time principal payments will begin. It is expected that
$40.0 million will be retired in 2014-2015 from Entrance Fees with the
remaining balance paid off over the term of the bonds from on going
operations.



In 2007 and 2008 we extended a line of credit from our Strategic
Reserve Fund to the Acacia Creek Board of Directors for both the
Union City and Covina campuses. With the Union City Financing

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

224

completed in February, 2008, the amount owed under the Union City
Line of Credit was paid back out of the proceeds of the bond issue. We
anticipate the Covina Project Financing will be completed in
November of this year at which time all amounts borrowed under the
Covina Line of Credit will also be paid in full from the bond proceeds.
The terms of the Lines of Credit call for a payback at the time of the
financing with terms comparable to our average investment returns.


In late FY 2007 we applied to become a certified provider with
Medicare and Medi-Cal for our skilled nursing unit on our Union City
campus. In 2008 we were certified. In so doing, we have generated
new revenue in 2008 in the amount of $786K and have budgeted
$1.385 million in 2009. This program will allow our residents to
access the benefits to which they are entitled and will significantly
extend the reach of our charitable dollars.



Consistent with the senior living industry and the business community
at large, we froze the California Masonic Retirement 403(b) plan
effective March 31, 2007. All participants were deemed 100% vested
upon termination of that Plan. As of June 30, 2008 the 403(b) plan
remains frozen.



As a result of changes to the CMC adopted at the 2006 Annual
Communication, a separate audit committee of the Grand Lodge was
formed. At the Finance Committee’s recommendation, the MHC
Board delegated the audit function to the GL audit committee.



In 2008 we expanded our Masonic Outreach Services Program. In
2007 we served 100 clients and in 2008 146 clients were served. This
increased outreach relieved our constituents who are on the Masonic
Homes waiting list or who are unable to apply for admission.



One of the Grand Master’s projects in 2008 was the expansion of our
Family Resource Center programming. One of our children’s homes
was converted to accommodate more clients. The clients served in
2008 were approximately 109 compared to 63 in 2007. The purpose of
the program is to facilitate the reunification of families as well as
strengthen families who are struggling to stay together.



Campus master planning continues as an ongoing initiative, with the
updating of the overall plan relating to our frontage property at Union
City.
